Screening of Antiviral Agents Against CHIKV Using Reporter Virus.

Authors: Castro EF, Alvarez D
Original abstract (English)
Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) causes a disease characterized by chronic musculoskeletal inflammation for which specific antivirals are not yet available. Currently, a supportive therapy to alleviate fever and pain is used, but it does not limit viral replication or the persistence of chronic arthritis symptoms. Thus, the identification and development of new active molecules against CHIKV is urgently needed. Here, we present a cell-based methodology that enables the implementation of a rapid and cost-effective strategy for high- and medium-throughput screening (HTS) of compounds, including repurposed drugs or novel molecules. This methodology allows for the identification of novel antiviral hits with a good activity and selectivity profile against CHIKV.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) causes a disease characterized by chronic musculoskeletal inflammation for which specific antivirals are not yet available. Currently, a supportive therapy to alleviate fever and pain is used, but it does not limit viral replication or the persistence of chronic arthritis symptoms.
Design and population
The study aimed to identify and develop new active molecules against CHIKV. The research focused on the development of a cell-based methodology for high- and medium-throughput screening (HTS) of compounds.
Core methods
The study employed a cell-based methodology to implement a rapid and cost-effective strategy for high- and medium-throughput screening (HTS) of compounds, including repurposed drugs or novel molecules.
Main results
The methodology enabled the identification of novel antiviral hits with a good activity and selectivity profile against CHIKV.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as a demonstration of the potential of their methodology to identify effective antiviral agents against CHIKV. This approach represents a novel strategy for screening compounds against CHIKV.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of new antiviral therapies for CHIKV, which could help manage the disease more effectively and reduce the burden of chronic musculoskeletal inflammation.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to presenting a methodology and its potential for identifying antiviral agents, without reporting specific results or validation. The evidence is moderate as it is based on a proposed strategy rather than confirmed outcomes.

表位标记和免疫共沉淀鉴定病毒蛋白相互作用物。

Epitope Tagging and Coimmunoprecipitation to Identify Viral Protein Interactors.
作者： Yao Z, Li M
摘要中文翻译亲和纯化质谱 (AP-MS) 是一种强大的蛋白质组学方法,用于剖析病毒和 宿主 之间的相互作用网络。传统的 AP-MS 采用病毒蛋白的过度表达作为诱饵来丰富 宿主 相互作用子。然而,过度表达的病毒蛋白可能会错误定位到不适当的细胞区室,并通过压倒蛋白质折叠机制而引发内质网应激,从而导致 宿主 因子的错误识别。为了克服这些限制,我们引入了一种基于直接感染表位标记的 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV/myc-E2) 的 AP-MS 策略,我们用它成功地发现了 CHIKV 细胞 储存宿主 巨噬细胞中的两个新的 抗病毒药物 因子。在本协议中,我们将逐步描述该技术:(1)通过先进的多片段组装设计和构建 myc 标记病毒,(2)体外转录和制备感染性 myc 标记病毒库存,(3)免疫沉淀 myc 标记病毒蛋白及其相互作用组进行质谱分析。该策略能够在生理相关背景下准确识别病毒相互作用因子,为未来使用标记病毒的蛋白质组学研究提供框架。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在通过开发一种更准确的策略来识别生理相关背景下的病毒蛋白相互作用物,从而解决传统 AP-MS 方法的局限性。这一背景凸显了了解病毒-宿主 相互作用对于 抗病毒药物 研究的重要性。
设计与对象
该研究引入了基于直接感染表位标记的 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV/myc-E2) 的 AP-MS 策略,以发现 CHIKV 细胞 储存宿主(特别是巨噬细胞)中的新 抗病毒药物 因子。
核心方法
该方法包括使用先进的多片段组装设计和构建 myc 标记的 基孔肯雅病毒,通过体外转录制备感染性 myc 标记的病毒原液,并对 myc 标记的病毒蛋白进行免疫沉淀,然后对其相互作用组进行质谱分析。
主要结果
该研究使用所描述的 AP-MS 策略成功地鉴定了 CHIKV 感染的巨噬细胞中的两个新的 抗病毒药物 因子。
解释与创新
作者将他们的发现解释为证明了他们的新型 AP-MS 策略在生理相关背景下准确识别病毒相互作用物的有效性,这为未来使用标记病毒的蛋白质组学研究提供了框架。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为研究病毒-宿主 相互作用提供了方法学上的进步,可以为未来对 抗病毒药物 因子的研究提供信息,并可能有助于 基孔肯雅病毒 感染靶向治疗的开发。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于对该方法及其应用的抽象描述,由于缺乏详细的结果和统计分析,证据强度中等。

Epitope Tagging and Coimmunoprecipitation to Identify Viral Protein Interactors.

Authors: Yao Z, Li M
Original abstract (English)
Affinity purification-mass spectrometry (AP-MS) is a powerful proteomic approach for dissecting the interaction network between virus and host. Traditional AP-MS employs overexpression of viral proteins as baits to enrich host interactors. However, overexpressed viral proteins may mislocalize to inappropriate cellular compartments and trigger endoplasmic reticulum stress by overwhelming the protein-folding machinery, which leads to false identification of host factors. To overcome these limitations, we introduce an AP-MS strategy based on direct infection with an epitope-tagged chikungunya virus (CHIKV/myc-E2), which we used to successfully uncover two new antiviral factors in CHIKV cellular reservoirs-macrophages. In this protocol, we will describe this technique step by step: (1) design and construction of myc-tagged virus by advanced multi-fragment assembly, (2) in vitro transcription and preparation of infectious myc-tagged virus stocks, and (3) immunoprecipitation of myc-tagged viral protein and its interactome for mass spectrometry analysis. This strategy enables accurate identification of viral interactors in a physiologically relevant context, providing a framework for future proteomic studies using tagged viruses.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the limitations of traditional AP-MS methods by developing a more accurate strategy for identifying viral protein interactors in a physiologically relevant context. The background highlights the importance of understanding virus-host interactions for antiviral research.
Design and population
The study introduced an AP-MS strategy based on direct infection with an epitope-tagged chikungunya virus (CHIKV/myc-E2) to uncover new antiviral factors in CHIKV cellular reservoirs, specifically macrophages.
Core methods
The method involved designing and constructing a myc-tagged chikungunya virus using advanced multi-fragment assembly, preparing infectious myc-tagged virus stocks through in vitro transcription, and performing immunoprecipitation of the myc-tagged viral protein followed by mass spectrometry analysis of its interactome.
Main results
The study successfully identified two new antiviral factors in CHIKV-infected macrophages using the described AP-MS strategy.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted their findings as demonstrating the effectiveness of their novel AP-MS strategy in accurately identifying viral interactors in a physiologically relevant context, which provides a framework for future proteomic studies using tagged viruses.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings offer a methodological advancement for studying virus-host interactions, which can inform future research on antiviral factors and potentially contribute to the development of targeted therapies for chikungunya virus infections.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to an abstract-only description of the method and its application, and the evidence strength is moderate due to the lack of detailed results and statistical analysis.

质粒分子工程的无限制克隆方法。

A Restriction-Free Cloning Approach for Molecular Engineering of Plasmids.
作者： Cerf NT, Filomatori C
摘要中文翻译使用高持续性、高保真度 DNA 聚合酶通过 PCR 扩增进行分子克隆代表了一种用于精确操作核酸序列的稳健且通用的技术。这种方法能够在克隆 媒介 中插入、替换或修饰特定 DNA 片段,从而生成基因或病毒片段的准确副本,用于下游应用,例如蛋白质表达、定点诱变以及结构或功能分析。使用持续的高保真聚合酶可显着减少碱基替换错误的发生,确保整个扩增过程中的序列完整性。传统上,限制性内切酶被用来促进定向克隆。然而,替代方法允许诱变,而不需要独特和特定的限制位点,并且可以应用于几乎任何克隆或无缝 DNA 组装策略。在本章中,我们描述了一种基于无限制性内切酶和无连接 PCR 的方案,广泛适用于任何环状 媒介。该方法能够对 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 基因组 进行定向诱变,为生成适合病毒学和分子研究的突变体构建体提供快速、高效和可靠的策略。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在开发一种不使用限制性酶的质粒分子工程方法,满足对更灵活、更有效的基因操作克隆技术的需求。
设计与对象
该研究描述了一种适用于任何环状 媒介 的无限制和无连接 PCR 方案,重点关注生成 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 基因组 突变体构建体的分子克隆过程。
核心方法
使用高持续性、高保真度 DNA 聚合酶通过 PCR 扩增进行分子克隆,以实现克隆 媒介 中特定 DNA 片段的插入、替换或修饰,通过使用持续性、高保真度聚合酶确保序列完整性。
主要结果
该方法允许对 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 基因组 进行定向诱变,为生成适合病毒学和分子研究的突变体构建体提供快速、有效和可靠的策略。
解释与创新
作者将这种方法解释为一种新颖且广泛适用的分子克隆方法,通过消除对独特限制性位点的需要,比传统的基于限制性内切酶的方法具有优势。
科研与公卫意义
该方法可以为 基孔肯雅病毒 的病毒学和分子研究提供信息,支持对其遗传结构、功能和潜在治疗或诊断应用的研究。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据没有报告局限性或证据强度,但该方法被描述为稳健且通用的,并且基于其详细描述和对 CHIKV 的应用进行了高置信度评估。

A Restriction-Free Cloning Approach for Molecular Engineering of Plasmids.

Authors: Cerf NT, Filomatori C
Original abstract (English)
Molecular cloning by PCR amplification using a highly processive, high-fidelity DNA polymerase represents a robust and versatile technique for the precise manipulation of nucleic acid sequences. This approach enables the insertion, replacement, or modification of specific DNA fragments within a cloning vector, thereby generating an accurate copy of a gene or viral segment for downstream applications, such as protein expression, site-directed mutagenesis, and structural or functional analyses. The use of processive, high-fidelity polymerases significantly reduces the occurrence of base substitution errors, ensuring sequence integrity throughout the amplification process. Traditionally, restriction enzymes have been employed to facilitate directional cloning; however, alternative methods allow for mutagenesis without the need for unique and specific restriction sites and can be applied to virtually any cloning or seamless DNA assembly strategy. In this chapter, we describe a restriction enzyme-free and ligation-free PCR-based protocol widely applicable to any circular vector. This method enables targeted mutagenesis of the chikungunya virus (CHIKV) genome, offering a fast, efficient, and reliable strategy for generating mutant constructs suitable for virological and molecular studies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop a method for molecular engineering of plasmids without the use of restriction enzymes, addressing the need for a more flexible and efficient cloning technique for genetic manipulation.
Design and population
The study describes a restriction-free and ligation-free PCR-based protocol applicable to any circular vector, focusing on the molecular cloning process for generating mutant constructs of the chikungunya virus (CHIKV) genome.
Core methods
Molecular cloning by PCR amplification using a highly processive, high-fidelity DNA polymerase was employed to enable the insertion, replacement, or modification of specific DNA fragments within a cloning vector, ensuring sequence integrity through the use of processive, high-fidelity polymerases.
Main results
The method allows for targeted mutagenesis of the chikungunya virus (CHIKV) genome, providing a fast, efficient, and reliable strategy for generating mutant constructs suitable for virological and molecular studies.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret this method as a novel and widely applicable approach for molecular cloning, offering advantages over traditional restriction enzyme-based methods by eliminating the need for unique restriction sites.
Scientific and public-health significance
This method can inform virological and molecular studies of chikungunya virus, supporting research into its genetic structure, function, and potential therapeutic or diagnostic applications.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report limitations or evidence strength, but the method is described as robust and versatile, with a high confidence assessment based on its detailed description and application to CHIKV.

土耳其阿达纳省更新蚊科(双翅目)名录,首次记录白纹伊蚊

Updated checklist of Culicidae (Diptera) in Adana Province, Türkiye, with the first record of Aedes albopictus
作者： Hakan Kavur, Davut Alptekın, Kavur H, Alptekin D., Davut Alptekin
摘要中文翻译<title>Abstract</title> <p> <italic>白纹伊蚊 (Ae.) albopictus</italic> 是一种引起重大公共卫生问题的入侵性蚊子,因为它能够传播登革热、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒等虫媒病毒。尽管其在欧洲和土耳其的扩张已有充分记录,但来自东地中海地区的数据仍然有限。本研究旨在更新阿达纳省蚊科动物区系,并确定蚊科的发生和定居状况。白纹伊蚊</斜体>。2024 年夏季,我们在土耳其阿达纳省的城市、城郊、农业和沿海栖息地的 30 个地点进行了实地调查。使用 CDC 微型诱光器收集成年蚊子,同时从水生栖息地采集未成熟阶段的蚊子,并在实验室条件下饲养至成年。使用标准形态键和分类学参考进行物种鉴定。总共鉴定了 2,130 个标本,代表 7 个蚊种和 4 个属。<italic>淡色库蚊 (Cx.) pipiens</italic> 是优势种,而 <italic>Ae. 是优势种。白纹伊蚊共227只(10.64%)。在所有调查地区(Seyhan、Yureğir、Karataş 和 Yumurtalık)均发现了该物种。现场收集的成虫和实验室出现的标本均证实了所有发育阶段的存在,其中塞伊汉区的丰度较高,表明存在局部种群聚集地。多个生命阶段的检测为Ae的建立提供了有力的证据。阿达纳省的白纹伊蚊,代表着土耳其向南的显着扩张。这些发现填补了国家分布数据的一个关键空白,并突显了东地中海地区持续的入侵动态。由于其流行病学重要性,需要加强 监测 和综合 媒介 管理。</p>
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在更新阿达纳省蚊科动物区系并确定白蚊的发生和定植状况。这项研究的动机是与AE相关的公共卫生问题。白纹伊蚊因其能够传播登革热、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒等虫媒病毒。
设计与对象
2024 年夏季,在土耳其阿达纳省的城市、城郊、农业和沿海栖息地的 30 个地点进行了实地调查。该研究的重点是识别蚊子种类并评估伊蚊的存在情况。白纹伊蚊在这些不同的环境中。
核心方法
使用 CDC 微型诱光器收集成年蚊子,从水生栖息地采集未成熟阶段的蚊子,并在实验室条件下饲养至成年。使用标准形态键和分类学参考进行物种鉴定。
主要结果
总共鉴定了 2,130 个标本,代表 7 个蚊种和 4 个属。白纹伊蚊占 227 例(10.64%),在所有调查地区(Seyhan、Yüreğir、Karataş 和 Yumurtalık)均检测到。
解释与创新
多个生命阶段的检测为Ae的建立提供了有力的证据。阿达纳省的白纹伊蚊,代表了土耳其向南的显着扩张。这些发现填补了国家分布数据的一个关键空白,并突显了东地中海地区持续的入侵动态。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果强调,由于伊蚊的流行病学重要性,需要加强 监测 和综合 媒介 管理。白纹伊蚊,可以传播基孔肯雅热和其他虫媒病毒。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于实地调查和实验室饲养,可能无法涵盖AE的所有方面。证据是中等的,因为它依赖于形态学鉴定,不包括 CHIKV 传播 的分子确认。

Updated checklist of Culicidae (Diptera) in Adana Province, Türkiye, with the first record of Aedes albopictus

Authors: Hakan Kavur, Davut Alptekın, Kavur H, Alptekin D., Davut Alptekin
Original abstract (English)
<title>Abstract</title> <p> <italic>Aedes (Ae.) albopictus</italic> is an invasive mosquito species of major public health concern due to its ability to transmit arboviruses such as dengue, chikungunya, and Zika. Although its expansion in Europe and Türkiye is well documented, data from the eastern Mediterranean region remain limited. This study aimed to update the <italic>Culicidae</italic> fauna of Adana Province and determine the occurrence and establishment status of <italic>Ae. albopictus</italic> . Field surveys were conducted at 30 sites across urban, peri-urban, agricultural, and coastal habitats in Adana Province, Türkiye, during the summer of 2024. Adult mosquitoes were collected using CDC miniature light traps, while immature stages were sampled from aquatic habitats and reared to adulthood under laboratory conditions. Species identification was performed using standard morphological keys and taxonomic references. A total of 2,130 specimens representing seven mosquito species and four genera were identified. <italic>Culex (Cx.) pipiens</italic> was the dominant species, whereas <italic>Ae. albopictus</italic> accounted for 227 individuals (10.64%). The species was detected in all surveyed districts (Seyhan, Yüreğir, Karataş, and Yumurtalık). Both field-collected adults and laboratory-emerged specimens confirmed the presence of all developmental stages, with higher abundance in Seyhan District indicating localized population foci. The detection of multiple life stages provides strong evidence for the establishment of <italic>Ae. albopictus</italic> in Adana Province, representing a significant southward expansion in Türkiye. These findings fill a critical gap in national distribution data and highlight ongoing invasion dynamics in the eastern Mediterranean region. Enhanced surveillance and integrated vector management are required due to its epidemiological importance. </p>
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to update the Culicidae fauna of Adana Province and determine the occurrence and establishment status of Ae. The research is motivated by the public health concern associated with Ae. albopictus due to its ability to transmit arboviruses such as dengue, chikungunya, and Zika.
Design and population
Field surveys were conducted at 30 sites across urban, peri-urban, agricultural, and coastal habitats in Adana Province, Türkiye, during the summer of 2024. The study focused on identifying mosquito species and assessing the presence of Ae. albopictus in these diverse environments.
Core methods
Adult mosquitoes were collected using CDC miniature light traps, while immature stages were sampled from aquatic habitats and reared to adulthood under laboratory conditions. Species identification was performed using standard morphological keys and taxonomic references.
Main results
A total of 2,130 specimens representing seven mosquito species and four genera were identified. albopictus accounted for 227 individuals (10.64%) and was detected in all surveyed districts (Seyhan, Yüreğir, Karataş, and Yumurtalık).
Interpretation and novelty
The detection of multiple life stages provides strong evidence for the establishment of Ae. albopictus in Adana Province, representing a significant southward expansion in Türkiye. These findings fill a critical gap in national distribution data and highlight ongoing invasion dynamics in the eastern Mediterranean region.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings highlight the need for enhanced surveillance and integrated vector management due to the epidemiological importance of Ae. albopictus, which can transmit chikungunya and other arboviruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on field surveys and laboratory rearing, which may not capture all aspects of Ae. The evidence is moderate as it relies on morphological identification and does not include molecular confirmation of CHIKV transmission.

白纹伊蚊种群的 CHIKV 和 DENV 来源和能力

Provenance and competence for CHIKV and DENV of Aedes albopictus populations
作者： Riccardo Piccinno, Yoann Madec, Marina Mariconti, Giulia Fiorenza, Davide Carraretto, Federico Forneris, Eleonora Flacio, Silvano Coletti, Giuliano Gasperi, Anna‐Bella Failloux
摘要中文翻译Aedes albopictus 是世界上最具危害性的入侵物种之一,近年来在新殖民地区越来越多地与基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)和登革病毒(DENV)的本地传播有关。自1980年代初入侵地中海盆地以来,不同谱系的反复引入导致了入侵种群中的广泛基因组混合。本研究探讨了这些多样化遗传背景的复杂动态如何影响 Aedes albopictus 对基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)、登革病毒(DENV)和寨卡病毒(ZIKV)的传播能力。我们使用高度多态的简单重复序列(SSR)标记对来自祖先种群(中国广州)和五个地中海地区十个入侵种群的 Aedes albopictus 样本进行了基因分型(南方瑞士、北/中意大利、巴尔干半岛和希腊)。通过贝叶斯结构(v2.3.2.0)和 DIYABC-RF 推断了遗传祖先和人口历史。利用感染率、传播效率和传播能力指数,结合单变量和多变量模型,评估了种群祖先与 Aedes albopictus 对 CHIKV(06.21)、DENV-1 和 ZIKV(PE243)的传播能力之间的关联。将历史记录与人口模型相结合表明,Aedes albopictus 在地中海地区的建立是由混乱且不连续的谱系引入和混合事件驱动的,导致了高空间和祖先异质性。值得注意的是,这种遗传异质性显著影响了 Aedes albopictus 对 CHIKV 和 DENV 的传播能力。有趣的是,具有不同祖先的邻近地理区域种群表现出截然不同的传播能力特征,而地理上较远但共享遗传祖先的种群则在 CHIKV 和 DENV 的传播能力上表现出相似性。我们的研究结果表明,Aedes albopictus 的复杂种群动态和殖民历史导致了传播能力的差异,从而改变了欧洲地区蚊媒病毒风险的地理格局。这些结果强调了在欧洲地区制定本地化、地理针对性的传播控制策略的紧迫性,因为该地区正面临输入性和本地传播蚊媒病毒感染的上升趋势。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究调查了不同遗传背景的复杂动态是否以及如何影响 媒介 对 CHIKV、DENV 和 ZIKV 病毒的能力的变化。白纹伊蚊是世界上最危险的入侵物种之一,在新殖民地区越来越多地与基孔肯雅热 (CHIKV) 和登革热 (DENV) 病毒的本土 传播 相关。
设计与对象
本研究分析了来自祖先种群(中国广州)和五个地中海地区十个入侵种群的 Aedes albopictus 样本(南方瑞士、北/中意大利、巴尔干半岛和希腊)。自1980年代初入侵地中海盆地以来,不同谱系的反复引入导致了入侵种群中的广泛基因组混合。
核心方法
使用感染率、传播效率和 传播 效率指数评估人群血统与 媒介 对 CHIKV (06.21)、DENV-1 和 ZIKV (PE243) 能力之间的关联,并通过单变量和多变量模型进行分析。使用贝叶斯结构 (v2.3.2.0) 和 DIYABC-RF 推断遗传祖先和人口统计历史。
主要结果
将历史记录与人口模型相结合表明,Ae 的建立。地中海的白纹伊蚊是由混乱、不连续的谱系引入和混合事件驱动的,导致了高度的空间和祖先异质性。值得注意的是,这种遗传异质性显着影响 媒介 的能力,特别是对于 CHIKV 和 DENV。
解释与创新
具有不同血统的邻近地理区域的人群表现出截然不同的能力特征,而具有遗传血统的地理遥远的人群则表现出类似的 媒介 对 CHIKV 和 DENV 的能力。我们的研究结果表明,伊蚊复杂的种群动态和定殖历史。白纹伊蚊导致 媒介 能力的变化,改变虫媒病毒风险的地理格局。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果强调了欧洲迫切需要本地化、按地理位置定制的 媒介 控制策略,该地区面临着输入性和本土虫媒病毒感染的上升趋势。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据并未报告研究设计或统计方法的具体局限性。由于依赖历史记录和人口模型,调查结果的可信度较低,这可能会带来不确定性。

Provenance and competence for CHIKV and DENV of Aedes albopictus populations

Authors: Riccardo Piccinno, Yoann Madec, Marina Mariconti, Giulia Fiorenza, Davide Carraretto, Federico Forneris, Eleonora Flacio, Silvano Coletti, Giuliano Gasperi, Anna‐Bella Failloux
Original abstract (English)
Aedes albopictus is one of the world’s most hazardous invasive species, increasingly implicated in the autochthonous transmission of Chikungunya (CHIKV) and Dengue (DENV) viruses in newly colonized areas. Since invading the Mediterranean Basin several in the early 1980s, recurrent introductions of distinct lineages have driven extensive genomic admixture within adventive populations. This study investigates whether and how the complex dynamics of these diverse genetic backgrounds have shaped variations in vector competence for CHIKV, DENV, and Zika (ZIKV) viruses. Using highly polymorphic simple sequence repeat (SSR) markers, we genotyped Ae. Albopictus samples from an ancestral population (Guangzhou, China) and ten adventive populations across five Mediterranean regions (Southern Switzerland, Northern/Central Italy, the Balkans, and Greece). Genetic ancestry and demographic histories were inferred using Bayesian STRUCTURE (v2.3.2.0) and DIYABC-RF. The association between population ancestry and vector competence for CHIKV (06.21), DENV-1, and ZIKV (PE243) was assessed using infection rate, dissemination efficiency, and transmission efficiency indices, analyzed via univariate and multivariate models. Combining historical records with demographic models revealed that the establishment of Ae. Albopictus in the Mediterranean was driven by chaotic, discontinuous lineage introductions and admixture events, resulting in high spatial and ancestry heterogeneity. Notably, this genetic heterogeneity significantly influences vector competence, in particular for CHIKV and DENV. Interestingly, populations in neighboring geographical areas with distinct ancestries exhibited drastically different competence profiles, whereas geographically distant populations sharing genetic ancestry displayed similar vector competence for CHIKV and DENV. Our findings demonstrate that the complex population dynamics and colonization history of Ae. Albopictus contributes to variation in vector competence, altering the geographical landscape of arboviral risk. These results underscore the urgent need for localized, geographically tailored vector control strategies in Europe, a region facing a rising trend of both imported and autochthonous arboviral infections.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study investigates whether and how the complex dynamics of diverse genetic backgrounds have shaped variations in vector competence for CHIKV, DENV, and ZIKV viruses. The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations of the study design or statistical methods. The confidence in the findings is moderate due to the reliance on historical records and demographic models, which may introduce uncertainty.

通过流式细胞术分析虫媒病毒感染组织的细胞群。

Analysis of Cell Populations from Arbovirus-Infected Tissues by Flow Cytometry.
作者： Martins JR, Batista VL, Queiroz-Junior CM, Teixeira MM, Costa VV
摘要中文翻译虫媒病毒仍然是世界许多地区公共卫生的重大挑战。尽管具有共同的流行病学特征,但每种虫媒病毒性疾病都表现出不同的细胞向性和激活模式,并发展为尚未完全阐明的免疫病理学结果。要更深入地了解这些过程,需要能够表征通常复杂的细胞反应的方法。在这种背景下,流式细胞术成为一种有价值的工具,可以表征异质细胞群、评估活化和增殖标记、量化炎症介质以及检测细胞死亡途径。通过整合这些参数,流式细胞术可以增强我们对虫媒病毒发病机制的了解,阐明其细胞向性,并了解免疫应答的感染情况。这篇方法论文章概述了将该技术应用于受基孔肯雅热、登革热、黄热病和寨卡病毒感染的不同目标组织的主要步骤,强调了最有效的策略。

Analysis of Cell Populations from Arbovirus-Infected Tissues by Flow Cytometry.

Authors: Martins JR, Batista VL, Queiroz-Junior CM, Teixeira MM, Costa VV
Original abstract (English)
Arboviruses still represent a major challenge to public health in many parts of the world. Despite shared epidemiological characteristics, each arboviral disorder displays distinct cellular tropism and activation patterns, progressing to immunopathological outcomes that remain not fully elucidated. A deeper understanding of these processes requires methodologies capable of characterizing the often-complex cellular responses. In this context, flow cytometry emerges as a valuable tool, enabling the characterization of heterogeneous cell populations, the assessment of activation and proliferation markers, the quantification of inflammatory mediators, and the detection of cell death pathways. By integrating these parameters, flow cytometry can enhance our understanding of arbovirus pathogenesis, clarify their cellular tropism, and understand immune responses to infection. This methodological article outlines the main steps for applying the technique to different target tissues infected by chikungunya, dengue, yellow fever, and Zika viruses, emphasizing the most effective strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Despite shared epidemiological characteristics, each arboviral disorder displays distinct cellular tropism and activation patterns, progressing to immunopathological outcomes that remain not fully elucidated.
Design and population
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the study design, setting, or study population.
Core methods
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the core experimental, analytical, or statistical methods.
Main results
The supplied evidence does not clearly state a directly observed main result.
Interpretation and novelty
By integrating these parameters, flow cytometry can enhance our understanding of arbovirus pathogenesis, clarify their cellular tropism, and understand immune responses to infection.
Scientific and public-health significance
Arboviruses still represent a major challenge to public health in many parts of the world.
Limitations and evidence strength
This methodological article outlines the main steps for applying the technique to different target tissues infected by chikungunya, dengue, yellow fever, and Zika viruses, emphasizing the most effective strategies.

Vertical oviposition preference of Aedes albopictus (Skuse, 1895) (Diptera: Culicidae) at different heights and seasonal periods in an urban forest fragment in Manaus, Amazonas, Brazil

Vertical oviposition preference of Aedes albopictus (Skuse, 1895) (Diptera: Culicidae) at different heights and seasonal periods in an urban forest fragment in Manaus, Amazonas, Brazil
作者： William Ribeiro da Silva, Adriano Nobre Arcos, F.A. Ferreira, Joelma Soares da Silva, Grafe Oliveira Pontes, Mário Antônio Navarro-Silva, Rosemary Aparecida Roque, João Antônio Cyrino Zequi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估白纹伊蚊在不同季节期的垂直产卵偏好,因为这些蚊子是多种虫媒病毒的重要媒介,包括引起登革热、寨卡病毒和基孔肯雅热的病毒。
设计与对象
研究在巴西马瑙斯市一个六层的城市森林碎片中的塔楼内进行,持续时间为10周,其中前5周为旱季,后5周为雨季。每个高度层安装三个卵 trap,总计每个采样设置30个卵 trap。
核心方法
使用卵 trap阳性指数和卵密度指数来比较不同高度层和不同季节间的种群模式。采样在特定高度进行,包括地面层、1.20米、2.50米、3.60米、4.90米、6.00米、7.30米、8.40米、9.70米和10.80米。
主要结果
卵的数量在较低高度处更为丰富。相对湿度与卵的平均数量呈正相关,尤其是在旱季期间。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,当条件适宜时,白纹伊蚊能够占据高处环境,强调了在媒介 监测项目中考虑垂直分布的重要性。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果可以为城市地区,特别是正在经历垂直扩展的地区,提供关于媒介 监测项目的参考,通过强调考虑垂直分布以制定有效的控制策略。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于对摘要的分析,证据具有普遍性,因此对垂直产卵模式的解释具有中等置信度。

Vertical oviposition preference of Aedes albopictus (Skuse, 1895) (Diptera: Culicidae) at different heights and seasonal periods in an urban forest fragment in Manaus, Amazonas, Brazil

Authors: William Ribeiro da Silva, Adriano Nobre Arcos, F.A. Ferreira, Joelma Soares da Silva, Grafe Oliveira Pontes, Mário Antônio Navarro-Silva, Rosemary Aparecida Roque, João Antônio Cyrino Zequi
Original abstract (English)
Mosquitoes of the genus Aedes Meigen, 1818 (Diptera: Culicidae) are important vectors of several arboviruses, including those that cause dengue, Zika, and chikungunya. Despite their epidemiological relevance, the vertical distribution of these species in large urban centers remains insufficiently documented. This study evaluated the vertical oviposition preference of Aedes albopictus (Skuse, 1895) at different heights during different seasonal periods. The research was conducted in a six-floor tower located within an urban forest fragment in Manaus, Brazil. The tower measured 15.13 m in height and 3.20 m at its base. Sampling was conducted at the following heights: ground level, 1.20 m, 2.50 m, 3.60 m, 4.90 m, 6.00 m, 7.30 m, 8.40 m, 9.70 m, and 10.80 m. Three ovitraps were installed at each height level, spaced 1.50 m apart, totaling 30 ovitraps per sampling. Monitoring was conducted daily over a 10-week period, with five weeks in the dry season and five weeks in the rainy season. The ovitrap positivity index and egg density index were used to compare population patterns among height levels and between seasons. Egg abundance was higher at lower heights. Relative humidity was positively associated with the mean number of eggs, particularly during the dry season. Although most eggs were recorded in the lower strata, their presence at higher levels indicates that Ae. albopictus is able to occupy elevated environments when conditions are suitable. These findings reinforce the need to consider vertical distribution in vector surveillance programs, especially in urban areas undergoing vertical expansion.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to evaluate the vertical oviposition preference of Aedes albopictus during different seasonal periods, as these mosquitoes are important vectors of several arboviruses, including those that cause dengue, Zika, and chikungunya.
Design and population
The study was conducted in a six-floor tower within an urban forest fragment in Manaus, Brazil, over a 10-week period with five weeks in the dry season and five weeks in the rainy season. Three ovitraps were installed at each height level, totaling 30 ovitraps per sampling.
Core methods
Ovitrap positivity index and egg density index were used to compare population patterns among height levels and between seasons. Sampling was conducted daily at specific heights: ground level, 1.20 m, 2.50 m, 3.60 m, 4.90 m, 6.00 m, 7.30 m, 8.40 m, 9.70 m, and 10.80 m.
Main results
Egg abundance was higher at lower heights. Relative humidity was positively associated with the mean number of eggs, particularly during the dry season.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings indicate that Aedes albopictus can occupy elevated environments when conditions are suitable, highlighting the importance of vertical distribution in vector surveillance programs.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results can inform vector surveillance programs in urban areas, especially those experiencing vertical expansion, by emphasizing the need to consider vertical distribution for effective control strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to an abstract-only analysis, and the evidence is general in nature, suggesting a moderate confidence level in the interpretation of vertical oviposition patterns.

2020年至2026年虫媒病毒风险变化及应对措施

Changes in arbovirus risks and responses from 2020 to 2026
作者： Zachary J. Madewell, Laura E. Adams, Sarah Anne J. Guagliardo, Anna Drexler, Roberto Barrera, Randall J. Nett, Gabriela Paz‐Bailey, Natalie E. Dean, G. Paz-Bailey
摘要中文翻译摘要 自 2020 年以来,通过蚊子和蠓传播的病毒已成为一个更大且更难以预测的公共卫生挑战。登革热继续造成最大的负担,但基孔肯雅热、奥罗普切热和其他病毒正在重新出现或传播到新的地区。在这篇叙述性综述中,我们总结了 2020 年至 2026 年虫媒病毒 流行病学、监测、诊断、疫苗接种、治疗、媒介 生态和蚊虫控制的重大变化。新工具可以帮助及早发现风险并预防疾病,但其有用性取决于当地能力、安全监测、可负担性、环境考虑和持续实施。虫媒病毒控制的下一阶段将取决于在 暴发 压垮卫生系统之前战略性地、公平地使用这些工具。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 2020 年至 2026 年虫媒病毒风险和应对措施的变化,特别检查 基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 及其相关疾病表现。核心问题是CHIKV在此期间在流行病学、监测、诊断、疫苗接种、治疗、媒介生态和蚊虫控制方面如何演变。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有文献的叙述性综合,总结了2020年至2026年虫媒病毒流行病学、监测、诊断、疫苗接种、治疗、媒介生态和蚊子控制的主要变化。它没有报告具体的数据库、检索日期或纳入研究的资格标准。
共识与结论
登革热继续造成最大的负担,但基孔肯雅热、奥罗普切热和其他病毒正在重新出现或传播到新的地区。该审查强调了虫媒病毒,特别是通过蚊子和蠓传播的虫媒病毒所带来的日益严重的公共卫生挑战。
争议与缺口
新工具在检测风险和预防疾病方面的有用性取决于当地的能力、安全监测、负担能力、环境考虑和持续实施。审查指出,这些因素在此类工具的应用中造成了证据差距和争议。
科研与实践启示
虫媒病毒控制的下一阶段将取决于在 暴发 压垮卫生系统之前战略性地、公平地使用这些工具。这表明需要有针对性的研究和实施策略来应对 CHIKV 和相关虫媒病毒的具体挑战。

Changes in arbovirus risks and responses from 2020 to 2026

Authors: Zachary J. Madewell, Laura E. Adams, Sarah Anne J. Guagliardo, Anna Drexler, Roberto Barrera, Randall J. Nett, Gabriela Paz‐Bailey, Natalie E. Dean, G. Paz-Bailey
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Since 2020, viruses spread by mosquitoes and biting midges have become a larger and less predictable public health challenge. Dengue continues to cause the greatest burden, but chikungunya, Oropouche, and other viruses are re-emerging or spreading into new areas. In this narrative review, we summarize major changes in arbovirus epidemiology, surveillance, diagnosis, vaccination, treatment, vector ecology, and mosquito control from 2020 to 2026. New tools can help detect risk earlier and prevent disease, but their usefulness depends on local capacity, safety monitoring, affordability, environmental considerations, and sustained implementation. The next phase of arbovirus control will depend on using these tools strategically, equitably, and before outbreaks overwhelm health systems.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on changes in arbovirus risks and responses from 2020 to 2026, specifically examining Chikungunya virus (CHIKV) and its associated disease manifestations. The central question is how CHIKV has evolved in terms of epidemiology, surveillance, diagnosis, vaccination, treatment, vector ecology, and mosquito control during this period.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of available literature, summarizing major changes in arbovirus epidemiology, surveillance, diagnosis, vaccination, treatment, vector ecology, and mosquito control from 2020 to 2026. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria for included studies.
Consensus and conclusions
Dengue continues to cause the greatest burden, but Chikungunya, Oropouche, and other viruses are re-emerging or spreading into new areas. The review highlights the increasing public health challenge posed by arboviruses, particularly those transmitted by mosquitoes and biting midges.
Controversies and gaps
The usefulness of new tools for detecting risk and preventing disease depends on local capacity, safety monitoring, affordability, environmental considerations, and sustained implementation. The review notes that these factors create evidence gaps and controversies in the application of such tools.
Research and practice implications
The next phase of arbovirus control will depend on using these tools strategically, equitably, and before outbreaks overwhelm health systems. This suggests a need for targeted research and implementation strategies to address the specific challenges of CHIKV and related arboviruses.

针对RNA病毒的广谱直接作用抗病毒药物抗病毒药物的发展方向

Towards broader-spectrum direct-acting antivirals against RNA viruses
作者： Hendrik Jan Thibaut, Dirk Jochmans, Christophe Van Dijck, Manon Laporte, Laurens Liesenborghs, Johan H. Neyts, Johan Neyts
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本评论聚焦于针对RNA病毒的广谱直接作用抗病毒药物(BSAs)的发展,特别强调了基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)。核心问题是:是否可以设计出针对多种RNA病毒属、科甚至跨科的BSAs,以及这种方法如何应对由RNA病毒引起的感染的治疗和预防挑战。
证据基础与方法
本评论是对抗病毒药物药物开发的系统分析,讨论了BSAs的合理性、其生物学决定因素以及所需的临床开发框架。它没有报告特定的数据库、搜索日期或研究数量,但提到了利用宏基因组学和高通量测序技术来扩展对病毒多样性的理解。本评论的结构旨在提供针对RNA病毒的批准和实验性抗病毒药物药物的非详尽概述。
共识与结论
本评论得出结论,开发BSAs对于应对当前未满足的医疗需求和加强流行病和大流行病的应对能力至关重要。它指出,新出现的病毒很可能属于已知的病毒科,BSAs可用于治疗和预防已知病毒引起的感染,特别是对于目前尚无抗病毒药物药物可用的病毒。本评论还强调了对已知病毒(如CHIKV)和新兴威胁(如SARS-CoV-2)的应对准备的重要性。
争议与缺口
本评论指出,一些抗病毒药物药物,如阿奇霉素、伊曲康唑、羟氯喹等,由于缺乏充分的临床前证据,被迅速用于临床试验,引发了对其疗效和安全性的担忧。此外,本评论还强调,'广谱' 抗病毒药物这一术语缺乏明确的定义,需要更精确的术语和框架来评估药物的抗病毒药物谱。本评论还指出,BSAs的开发仍处于早期阶段,需要更多的研究来识别和验证潜在靶点。
科研与实践启示
本评论建议,类似于用于HIV治疗的固定剂量联合疗法,抗病毒药物的固定剂量联合疗法可能是对抗RNA病毒的理想选择。它还强调了持续投资于抗病毒药物药物发现的重要性,特别是针对具有广泛宿主范围、跨物种传播能力以及快速传播潜力的病毒。本评论还指出,需要建立能够加速BSAs评估的临床开发框架,特别是在暴发的背景下。

Towards broader-spectrum direct-acting antivirals against RNA viruses

Authors: Hendrik Jan Thibaut, Dirk Jochmans, Christophe Van Dijck, Manon Laporte, Laurens Liesenborghs, Johan H. Neyts, Johan Neyts
Original abstract (English)
Nature Reviews Microbiology nature reviews microbiology https://doi.org/10.1038/s41579-026-01372-1 Review article Check for updates Towards broader-spectrum direct-acting antivirals against RNA viruses Hendrik Jan Thibaut 1, Dirk Jochmans 1,3, Christophe Van Dijck2,3, Manon Laporte 1,3, Laurens Liesenborghs 2 & Johan Neyts1 Abstract Small molecule antiviral drugs have transformed the management of infections such as those caused by HIV, hepatitis B virus, hepatitis C virus, herpesviruses and influenza viruses. In this Review, we discuss the rationale for building a diversified portfolio of broader-spectrum antivirals against RNA viruses with activity spanning multiple genera, entire families or even crossing viral families. e-mail: johan.neyts@kuleuven.be Nature Reviews Microbiology Review article Alongside vaccine development, sustained investment in antiviral drug discovery is therefore essential. In this Review, we discuss the rationale for the development of BSAs and provide a non-exhaustive overview of approved or experi- mental antiviral drugs active against viruses with an RNA genome. Most of these viruses are RNA viruses — the focus of this Review — including many emerging and clinically important pathogens10–12. Effective antiviral therapeutics remain unavailable for many other often life-threatening viral infections, many of which are caused by RNA viruses. The development of potent, safe and orally available antivirals is therefore essential both to address current unmet medical needs and to strengthen epidemic and pandemic preparedness. We provide a non-exhaustive overview of approved and investigational antiviral agents active against RNA viruses and discuss the biological determinants that underlie their antiviral spectrum. Finally, we outline the clinica...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the development of broader-spectrum direct-acting antivirals (BSAs) against RNA viruses, with a particular emphasis on Chikungunya virus (CHIKV). The central question is whether BSAs can be designed to target multiple RNA virus genera, families, or even cross families, and how this approach can address the challenges of treating and preventing viral infections caused by RNA viruses.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of antiviral drug development, discussing the rationale for BSAs, their biological determinants, and the clinical development frameworks required. It does not report specific databases, search dates, or study counts, but it does mention the use of metagenomics and high-throughput sequencing to expand understanding of viral diversity. The review is structured to provide a non-exhaustive overview of approved and experimental antiviral agents active against RNA viruses.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that the development of BSAs is essential to address current unmet medical needs and to strengthen epidemic and pandemic preparedness. It highlights that newly emerging viruses are likely to belong to known families, and that BSAs can be used for treatment and prophylaxis of infections with known viruses for which no antiviral treatment is available. The review also emphasizes the importance of preparedness against known viruses such as CHIKV and emerging threats like SARS-CoV-2.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that some antiviral drugs, such as azithromycin, itraconazole, hydroxychloroquine, and others, were rapidly tested in clinical trials with limited preclinical evidence, raising concerns about their efficacy and safety. Additionally, the review highlights that the term 'broad-spectrum' antiviral lacks a clear definition, and that there is a need for more precise terminology and frameworks to evaluate the antiviral spectrum of drugs. The review also points out that the development of BSAs is still in its early stages, and that more research is needed to identify and validate potential targets.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that fixed-dose combinations of antivirals, similar to those used in HIV treatment, could be ideal for combating RNA viruses. It also emphasizes the importance of sustained investment in antiviral drug discovery, particularly for viruses with a broad host range, capacity for zoonotic spillover, and rapid spread potential. The review highlights the need for clinical development frameworks that can accelerate the evaluation of BSAs, especially in the context of outbreaks.

针对中国虫媒病毒的保护性反应及对疫苗设计的启示:2026年展望

Protective responses and implications for vaccine designs against arboviruses in China: a 2026 perspective
作者： Yiu-Wing Kam, Shang-Yang Wu, Jia-Cheng Wu, Shan Wu, Jiachen Wu
摘要中文翻译本更新综述总结了近年来对人类免疫应答、发病机制和疫苗发展的理解进展,特别关注中国独特的流行病学情况。本综述识别了研究空白并提出了未来方向,强调需要多价疫苗、改进的诊断方法以及整合的监测系统。本综述聚焦于中国四种临床重要的虫媒病毒(DENV、ZIKV、JEV和CHIKV),探讨了近期对保护性免疫、发病机制和疫苗发展的理解进展,同时应对中国特有的流行病学挑战,包括多种登革热血清型的共循环、基孔肯雅热输入病例的增加,以及在气候变暖背景下对整合媒介 监测系统的需求[13,14,15,16]。摘要 虫媒病毒一直是公共卫生的持续挑战,其影响因气候变化、全球化和媒介扩展而加剧。截至2026年,中国面临由蚊媒病毒带来的不断演变的威胁,包括登革热(DENV)、寨卡病毒(ZIKV)、日本脑炎(JEV)和基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)。我们分析了临床结果,识别了关键的保护性免疫相关因素和疾病增强因素,并系统评估了2010年至2026年间疫苗候选疫苗。值得注意的是,美国食品药品监督管理局(FDA)批准的CHIKV疫苗Ixchiq是一个里程碑,但登革热和寨卡病毒的免疫策略仍面临挑战。我们讨论了中国在气候驱动的媒介扩展、跨境传播风险以及成功实施的JEV 疫苗接种项目中获得的关键经验教训。1 引言 虫媒病毒的出现和再出现继续对全球公共卫生构成重大威胁[1]。截至2026年中旬,中国面临由蚊媒病毒带来的不断演变的威胁,包括登革热(DENV)、寨卡病毒(ZIKV)、日本脑炎(JEV)和基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于中国四种临床重要的虫媒病毒(DENV、ZIKV、JEV和CHIKV),探讨了近期对保护性免疫、发病机制和疫苗发展的理解进展,同时应对中国特有的流行病学挑战,包括多种登革热血清型的共循环、基孔肯雅热输入病例的增加,以及在气候变暖背景下对整合媒介 监测系统的需求[13,14,15,16]。
证据基础与方法
综述包括2024年至2026年间发表的研究证据,特别关注中国特定的数据。它讨论了基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)的流行病学状况、血清型分布和临床表现,以及气候变化对媒介栖息地和疾病传播的影响。
共识与结论
CHIKV infections are characterized by acute fever lasting five to seven days, followed by persistent arthralgia that can last months to years. Chronic arthritis rates vary by study and population, with some reporting 13.3% of patients experiencing persistent arthralgia at 6 months post-infection [44], while others have documented chronic symptoms in 40–60% of patients at 12–24 months, depending on age, sex, and initial disease severity [7, 45].
争议与缺口
由于先前暴露模式的不同,基础免疫力的异质性对ADE风险和疫苗部署策略具有影响。此外,综述还强调了需要改进诊断方法和整合监测系统,以应对基孔肯雅病毒和其他虫媒病毒在中国不断演变的威胁。
科研与实践启示
综述强调需要多价疫苗、改进的诊断方法和整合的监测系统,以应对基孔肯雅病毒和其他虫媒病毒在中国带来的挑战。它还呼吁进一步研究血清型/谱系结构、复制动力学以及宿主免疫相互作用,这些因素塑造了疾病结果和公共卫生策略。

Protective responses and implications for vaccine designs against arboviruses in China: a 2026 perspective

Authors: Yiu-Wing Kam, Shang-Yang Wu, Jia-Cheng Wu, Shan Wu, Jiachen Wu
Original abstract (English)
This updated review synthesizes recent advancements in understanding human immune responses, pathogenesis, and vaccine development, with a particular focus on China’s unique epidemiological situation. This review identifies research gaps and proposes future directions, emphasizing the need for multivalent vaccines, improved diagnostics, and integrated surveillance systems. This review focuses on four clinically significant arboviruses (DENV, ZIKV, JEV, and CHIKV) in China, examining recent advances in understanding protective immunity, pathogenesis, and vaccine development while addressing China’s unique epidemiological challenges, including the co-circulation of multiple dengue serotypes, increasing chikungunya importation, and the need for integrated vector surveillance in a warming climate [13,14,15,16]. Abstract Arboviruses have remained a persistent public health challenge, with their impact intensified by climate change, globalization, and vector expansion. As of 2026, China faces evolving threats from mosquito-borne viruses, including Dengue (DENV), Zika (ZIKV), Japanese Encephalitis (JEV), and Chikungunya (CHIKV). We analyze clinical outcomes, identify key immune correlates of protection and disease enhancement, and systematically evaluate vaccine candidates from 2010 to 2026. Notably, the FDA-approved CHIKV vaccine Ixchiq represents a milestone, yet challenges remain for DENV and ZIKV immunization strategies. We discuss China’s evolving prevention strategies in the context of climate-driven vector expansion, cross-border transmission risks, and the critical lessons learned from the successful JEV vaccination program. 1 Introduction The emergence and reemergence of arboviruses continue to pose significant public health threats globally [1]. As of mid‐2026, the...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on four clinically significant arboviruses (DENV, ZIKV, JEV, and CHIKV) in China, examining recent advances in understanding protective immunity, pathogenesis, and vaccine development while addressing China’s unique epidemiological challenges, including the co-circulation of multiple dengue serotypes, increasing chikungunya importation, and the need for integrated vector surveillance in a warming climate [13,14,15,16].
Evidence base and method
The review includes evidence from studies published between 2024 and 2026, with a focus on China-specific data. It discusses the epidemiological landscape, serotype distribution, and clinical manifestations of CHIKV, as well as the impact of climate change on vector habitats and disease transmission.
Consensus and conclusions
CHIKV infections are characterized by acute fever lasting five to seven days, followed by persistent arthralgia that can last months to years. Chronic arthritis rates vary by study and population, with some reporting 13.3% of patients experiencing persistent arthralgia at 6 months post-infection [44], while others have documented chronic symptoms in 40–60% of patients at 12–24 months, depending on age, sex, and initial disease severity [7, 45].
Controversies and gaps
The heterogeneity in baseline immunity due to previous exposure patterns has implications for ADE risk and vaccine deployment strategies. Additionally, the review highlights the need for improved diagnostics and integrated surveillance systems to address the evolving threats posed by CHIKV and other arboviruses in China.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for multivalent vaccines, improved diagnostics, and integrated surveillance systems to address the challenges of CHIKV and other arboviruses in China. It also calls for further research into the serotype/lineage structure, replication kinetics, and host immune interactions that shape disease outcomes and public health strategies.

三种基孔肯雅病毒谱系,三种蚊媒策略:媒介适应性如何推动全球传播

Three Chikungunya Lineages, Three Mosquito Strategies: How Vector Adaptation Drives Global Spread
新闻简报由廖宇华及其同事发表在《PLOS Pathogens》上的一项新研究探讨了三种东/中/南非(ECSA)基因型基孔肯雅病毒亚谱系的分子进化。研究发现,每个亚谱系都独立演化出适应不同蚊媒媒介(如埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊)的策略,这可能解释了它们在不同地区的主导地位。该研究突出了基孔肯雅病毒在全球119个国家和地区传播的情况,最近在亚洲、欧洲和美洲出现了暴发。研究确定了ECSA-2.1亚谱系中结构蛋白基因的强化选择,该亚谱系与埃及伊蚊相关,表明该亚谱系正在积极完善其面向蚊媒媒介的分子工具包。这些发现对暴发预测、媒介控制以及疫苗开发具有重要意义,因为它们表明需要采取地区特异性且谱系敏感的策略。该研究未报告确认的暴发或病例,但提供了关于病毒进化机制及其未来传播潜力的见解。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年10月11日发表,讨论了基孔肯雅病毒在过去二十年中的全球传播。
地点与对象
分析涵盖了亚洲、欧洲、美洲以及南亚和东南亚等多个地区,重点关注基孔肯雅病毒及其亚谱系的传播情况。
事件
由廖宇华及其同事发表在《PLOS Pathogens》上的一项新研究分析了三种东/中/南非(ECSA)基因型基孔肯雅病毒亚谱系的分子进化,揭示了针对不同蚊媒媒介的适应性策略。
规模、影响与风险
该研究突出了基孔肯雅病毒在全球119个国家和地区传播的情况,特定亚谱系在不同地区占据主导地位。它确定了可能影响传播潜力的分子变化,并提出了对暴发预测、媒介控制以及疫苗开发的潜在影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究基于基因组和系统发育组学分析,其发现表明每个亚谱系都存在特定的分子适应性。研究提供了关于病毒进化策略的见解,但未报告确认的暴发或病例,而是侧重于分子和进化机制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Three Chikungunya Lineages, Three Mosquito Strategies: How Vector Adaptation Drives Global Spread

News Brief
A new study published in PLOS Pathogens by Yuhua Liao and colleagues explores the molecular evolution of three sub-lineages of the East/Central/South African (ECSA) genotype of Chikungunya virus. The research reveals that each sub-lineage has independently evolved strategies to adapt to different mosquito vectors, such as Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus, which may explain their regional dominance. The study highlights the global spread of Chikungunya virus across 119 countries and territories, with recent outbreaks in Asia, Europe, and the Americas. It identifies intensified selection on structural-protein genes in ECSA-2.1, which is associated with Aedes aegypti, and suggests that this sub-lineage has been actively refining its vector-facing molecular toolkit. The findings have implications for outbreak forecasting, vector control, and vaccine development, as they indicate the need for region-specific and lineage-aware strategies. The research does not report confirmed outbreaks or cases but provides insights into the virus's evolutionary mechanisms and potential for future spread.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Sunday, October 11, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Three Chikungunya Lineages, Three Mosquito Strategies: How Vector Adaptation Drives Global Spread by October 11, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Three Chikungunya Lineages, Three Mosquito Strategies: How Vector Adaptation Drives Global Spread Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Chikungunya virus has quietly become one of the most successful emerging pathogens of the past two decades, now reported across 119 countries and territories with recent outbreaks sweeping through Asia, Europe, and the Americas. Yet the virus has not spread as a single, uniform entity. Within the East/Central/South African genotype, which has increasingly predominated in multiple regions of the world, three distinct sub-lineages have risen to dominance in different geographic arenas. A new study published in PLOS Pathogens by Yuhua Liao, Jingqi Yang, Jingzhe Shang, Hangyu Zhou, and Aiping Wu offers a molecular explanation for this puzzling pattern, showing that each sub-lineage has independently evolved its own strategy for adapting to mosquito vectors, and that these divergent strategies underlie their regional competitive advantages. The research team assembled complete chikungunya virus genomes and applied comparative phylogenomic analyses to reconstruct the evolutionary history of the three ECSA sub-lineages: ECSA-IOL, ECSA-2.1, and ECSA-2.2. To probe how natural selection has acted on each lineage, they deployed a battery of selection pressure methods, including RELAX, BUSTED, and Contrast-FEL, which can detect intensification or relaxation of selective constraints across branches and identify individual codon sites under divergent selection between lineages. This combination allowed the authors to move beyond simple phylogenetic tree-building and ask a more mechanistic question: what molecular changes distinguish lineages that dominate one region from those that dominate another? The clearest and most consequential finding concerns ECSA-2.1, the sub-lineage responsible for the explosive displacement of the previously dominant Asian genotype across the Americas. The selection analyses revealed intensified selection acting specifically on the structural-protein genes of ECSA-2.1, the portion of the viral genome encoding the proteins that form the virion and interact directly with mosquito and host cells. Intensified selection on structural proteins is precisely the signature one would expect when a virus is under strong pressure to optimize its interactions with vector and host entry machinery, and the pattern suggests that this sub-lineage has been actively refining its vector-facing molecular toolkit. Crucially, ECSA-2.1 also accumulated substitutions previously associated with adaptation to Aedes aegypti, the primary urban mosquito vector of chikungunya in the Americas. This combination of intensified structural-protein selection and aegypti-associated mutations provides a coherent molecular account of the sub-lineage’s rapid takeover in the Western Hemisphere. Aedes aegypti is a highly efficient, human-adapted mosquito that thrives in dense urban environments, and a viral lineage optimized for this vector gains access to the dense, interconnected human populations that fuel large-scale epidemics. The study thus links a documented epidemiological transition, the replacement of the Asian genotype across the Americas, to specific and measurable evolutionary changes in the viral genome. ECSA-IOL, the Indian Ocean Lineage, tells a different evolutionary story. Rather than specializing on a single vector, this sub-lineage appears to have undergone a strategy switch, shifting its adaptation from Aedes albopictus toward Aedes aegypti. This transition is historically resonant: the original Indian Ocean outbreak of 2005 to 2006 was famously associated with a single alanine-to-valine substitution in the viral E1 envelope protein that enhanced transmission by Aedes albopictus, the Asian tiger mosquito. The new analysis suggests that ECSA-IOL subsequently broadened or redirected its vector competence, and this flexibility has allowed it to maintain its dominance across South and Southeast Asia, regions where both Aedes species circulate and where aegypti-adapted transmission offers access to the largest human populations. The third sub-lineage, ECSA-2.2, presents yet another adaptive profile. According to the study, ECSA-2.2 convergently acquired Aedes albopictus adaptation, arriving at vector-associated changes similar to those of other lineages through independent evolutionary routes, and layered additional envelope protein mutations on top of this convergent adaptation. Convergence is a powerful signal in evolutionary biology: when distinct lineages independently arrive at similar molecular solutions, it strongly suggests that those solutions confer a genuine selective advantage. The additional envelope mutations carried by ECSA-2.2 suggest, in the authors’ interpretation, an enhanced adaptive potential, a lineage that may be poised for further expansion should ecological conditions favor its vector associations. Taken together, the genome-wide analysis identified both lineage-characteristic substitutions, mutations unique to each sub-lineage, and convergent substitutions, mutations shared across lineages that arrived independently. These molecular signatures collectively constitute what the authors describe as markers associated with the competitive advantage of the ECSA genotype. The concept that emerges is one of vector niche partitioning: rather than competing head-to-head in a single ecological arena, the three sub-lineages have effectively divided the mosquito-vector landscape among themselves, with each lineage dominating regions where its particular vector adaptation is most advantageous. This reframes the global expansion of chikungunya not as a single wave of conquest but as three parallel evolutionary experiments, each tuned to a different vector ecology. The implications of this work extend well beyond evolutionary theory. For outbreak forecasting, the identification of lineage-characteristic and convergent molecular signatures provides a potential early-warning system: surveillance teams could monitor circulating viral populations for the appearance of vector-associated substitutions that presage a shift in transmission potential. For vector control, the findings suggest that prioritization strategies may need to be region-specific and lineage-aware, since the mosquito species that matters most for transmission depends on which sub-lineage is circulating. A control campaign designed around Aedes aegypti would be well matched to the Americas under ECSA-2.1 dominance but might need to account for Aedes albopictus in regions where ECSA-2.2 or albopictus-adapted variants are established. The study also carries weight for vaccine development. Chikungunya vaccine candidates currently in development or recently approved must contend with substantial antigenic and genetic diversity across the virus’s global population. If different sub-lineages carry distinct envelope protein mutations shaped by divergent vector adaptations, a vaccine designed against one lineage may offer reduced protection against another. The authors explicitly note that their findings have potential implications for the design of broadly protective vaccines, and the molecular map of lineage-specific and convergent substitutions they provide could help immunologists select vaccine strains or antigens that cover the full breadth of circulating diversity. More broadly, the study contributes to a growing appreciation that the epidemiology of arboviruses cannot be separated from the evolutionary dynamics of vector adaptation. Chikungunya, dengue, Zika, and yellow fever all share Aedes mosquito vectors, and the interplay between viral mutation and vector competence shapes which viruses flourish where. By demonstrating that three closely related sub-lineages of a single genotype have pursued three distinct adaptive strategies, Liao and colleagues provide a template for analyzing other arboviral systems and a reminder that the next geographic shift in chikungunya’s distribution may be foreshadowed in its genome long before it appears in clinic-based surveillance. As outbreaks continue to expand into new territories, watching the virus’s molecular evolution in the context of its mosquito partners may prove as important as counting human cases. 巴西贫民窟蚊虫高发区揭示基孔肯雅热风险集中区域 - Bioengineer.org

Mosquito Hotspots in a Brazilian Slum Reveal Where Chikungunya Risk Concentrates - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报一项发表于《PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases》的新研究绘制了巴西萨尔瓦多市一个非正式聚居区中基孔肯雅病毒的风险地图,揭示了环境和社会经济因素如何影响传播。该研究于2021年10月至2022年6月期间进行,结合了194个卵 trap 的昆虫学数据与888名5岁以上居民的血清学检测。研究发现,住宅周边区域,如未铺装的庭院和垃圾堆,蚊虫卵数量更高,表明这些区域是理想的繁殖场所。社会经济因素如食物不安全和住房质量差也与风险增加相关。该研究指出,老年人和居住在非正式住房中的居民感染率更高,表明累积暴露和防护措施不足。通过整合卫星温度数据和当地蚊虫数量,该研究为精准公共卫生提供了方法学上的进展,强调了在脆弱社区中实施针对性干预的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2021年10月至2022年6月期间进行,新闻于2026年10月12日发布。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于巴西萨尔瓦多市的一个非正式聚居区,涉及5岁以上888名居民。
事件
一项高分辨率研究通过分析蚊虫繁殖模式和人类血清阳性率,绘制了巴西贫民窟中基孔肯雅病毒的风险地图,揭示了社会经济和环境因素如何影响传播。
规模、影响与风险
该研究确定了蚊虫卵数量较高的住宅周边区域,并发现食物不安全、未铺装庭院和垃圾密度等因素是风险增强因素。还发现老年人和居住在非正式住房中的居民感染率更高。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用贝叶斯空间统计学和INLA分析数据,结合了卫星温度数据和卵 trap 卵数量。研究结果通过可信区间进行校准,并建议卵 trap 作为一种有用的监测工具,用于指导干预措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mosquito Hotspots in a Brazilian Slum Reveal Where Chikungunya Risk Concentrates - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A new study published in PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases has mapped the risk of chikungunya virus in an informal settlement in Salvador, Brazil, revealing how environmental and socioeconomic factors influence transmission. Conducted between October 2021 and June 2022, the research combined entomological data from 194 ovitraps with serological testing of 888 residents aged five and older. It found that peridomestic spaces, such as unpaved yards and garbage piles, had higher mosquito egg counts, indicating ideal breeding conditions. Socioeconomic factors like food insecurity and poor housing quality were also linked to increased risk. The study highlights that elderly residents and those in informal housing had higher odds of prior infection, suggesting cumulative exposure and reduced protective measures. By integrating satellite temperature data and local mosquito abundance, the research provides a methodological advance for precision public health, emphasizing the need for targeted interventions in vulnerable communities.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Monday, October 12, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Mosquito Hotspots in a Brazilian Slum Reveal Where Chikungunya Risk Concentrates by October 12, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Mosquito Hotspots in a Brazilian Slum Reveal Where Chikungunya Risk Concentrates Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In the crowded informal settlements of Salvador, Brazil, the risk of catching chikungunya is not spread evenly from street to street. It clusters around unpaved yards, piles of garbage, and the shaded peridomestic spaces where the yellow fever mosquito, Aedes aegypti, finds ideal conditions to breed. A new high-resolution study published in PLOS Neglected Tropical Diseases has mapped these fine-scale vulnerabilities with unusual precision, combining mosquito egg counts, household surveys, serological testing, and Bayesian spatial statistics to reveal exactly where the virus finds its footholds in one low-income community. The research, led by Jaqueline S. Cruz, Hussein Khalil, and colleagues working with long-standing infectious disease programs in Salvador, set out to answer a deceptively simple question: which socioeconomic and environmental factors determine both where Aedes aegypti mosquitoes flourish and which residents show evidence of past chikungunya virus infection? To do so, the team spent months embedded in a vulnerable informal community, deploying ovitraps and drawing blood samples from residents between October 2021 and June 2022. The result is one of the most granular portraits yet of how urban poverty, ecology, and viral transmission intersect at the scale of individual households. Chikungunya virus, or CHIKV, is an arbovirus transmitted primarily by Aedes mosquitoes, the same vectors responsible for dengue and Zika. First identified in Tanzania in the 1950s, the virus has swept through tropical and subtropical regions in successive waves, causing explosive epidemics characterized by fever and often debilitating joint pain that can persist for months or years. In Brazil, incidence has risen sharply in recent years, with the burden falling disproportionately on socioeconomically marginalized neighborhoods where irregular water supply, poor waste collection, and unpaved surfaces create abundant breeding habitat. Despite this clear ecological logic, the microscale drivers of transmission within informal settlements have remained poorly quantified, largely because most surveillance operates at municipal or district levels too coarse to capture household-level heterogeneity. Discover more Bioengineering News Subscription Bioengineering careers guide Bioengineering news subscription Bioengineer news subscription Bioengineering career guide To overcome this limitation, the researchers combined two complementary strands of evidence. The first was entomological: 194 ovitraps, simple containers holding water and a wooden paddle where female Aedes aegypti deposit their eggs, were distributed across the study area and monitored regularly. Egg counts per trap served as a proxy for vector abundance, capturing the spatial texture of mosquito proliferation at resolutions far finer than conventional indices. The second was serological: the team enrolled 888 residents aged five years and older, collecting blood samples to test for antibodies against chikungunya virus, alongside detailed questionnaires covering sociodemographic characteristics, housing conditions, and environmental exposures. Analyzing these data required statistical machinery capable of handling both spatial autocorrelation and multiple sources of uncertainty. The team employed Integrated Nested Laplace Approximation, or INLA, a Bayesian computational framework well suited to spatial modeling, to fit models linking ovitrap egg counts and individual seropositivity to their candidate predictors. Because the models report credible intervals rather than single point estimates, the findings convey a calibrated picture of how confident one can be in each association, an important safeguard when working with complex, noisy community data. Discover more Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges The entomological results were striking. Ovitraps placed in peridomicile locations, the spaces immediately surrounding homes such as yards and alleys, recorded substantially higher egg counts than traps in other settings, with an incidence rate ratio of 1.99 and a 95 percent credible interval spanning 1.31 to 3.06. In other words, simply being near a house nearly doubled the expected mosquito egg density, implicating the shaded, cluttered, and water-holding niches around dwellings as primary breeding grounds. Households reporting food insecurity, a marker of deep socioeconomic deprivation, also showed higher egg counts around their properties, as did homes with unpaved yards and locations surrounded by greater garbage density, which carried a risk elevation of 25 percent per unit increase. The serological analysis told a parallel story about human exposure. Among the 888 participants, several factors were independently associated with testing positive for chikungunya antibodies. Elderly residents faced roughly double the odds of seropositivity compared with younger adults, a pattern consistent with cumulative lifetime exposure and possibly with reduced access to protective behaviors such as air conditioning or intact window screens. Housing quality emerged as a powerful signal: living in a home with uncovered brick walls, a hallmark of unfinished informal construction, doubled the odds of prior infection, while having a yard, whether paved or unpaved, was associated with twofold to nearly threefold increases in seropositivity. Residents who reported personally seeing mosquitoes in or around their homes showed 94 percent higher odds of seropositivity, suggesting that individual perception tracks real exposure reasonably well. Discover more Bioengineering jobs listing iGEM competition updates Bioengineering research papers Bioengineering events calendar Bioengineering video tutorials Intriguingly, the model also incorporated remotely sensed satellite data, specifically Land Surface Temperature, and the spatially smoothed ovitrap egg counts as continuous covariates of seropositivity. Both variables showed positive associations with point estimates that did not exclude the null in the credible intervals, but their inclusion demonstrates a methodological advance: temperature measured from orbit at microscale resolution, and local mosquito abundance itself, can be layered into risk models alongside household characteristics. Warmer microclimates accelerate mosquito development and viral replication, and the authors argue that such fine-scale thermal variation deserves attention in future control planning, particularly as urban heat islands intensify. Taken together, the findings paint a coherent picture of chikungunya risk as a product of converging vulnerabilities. The same environmental degradations that concentrate mosquito breeding, unpaved ground, accumulated refuse, and precarious construction, also coincide with the socioeconomic conditions, food insecurity, elderly residents, and unfinished housing, that heighten human exposure. Crucially, the spatial overlap between ovitrap hotspots and seropositive clusters suggests that where mosquitoes are most abundant, residents are indeed being infected, validating ovitraps as a cheap and actionable surveillance tool that could direct interventions before outbreaks ignite. The authors conclude that effective chikungunya control in informal settlements cannot rely on generic campaigns alone. Instead, strategies must be tailored to the neighborhood scale: upgrading basic urban infrastructure, clearing garbage, paving and managing yards, targeting breeding sites around homes where egg densities peak, engaging communities in mosquito-source reduction, and incorporating microscale temperature data into risk maps. In an era of expanding arboviral threats across the tropics, this study offers a blueprint for precision public health, showing that with careful spatial epidemiology, the invisible geography of risk in a vulnerable community can be made visible, and ultimately, actionable. Mosquitoes, Climate Data Could Give East Africa Early Warning of Chikungunya - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org于2026年10月10日发表的文章讨论了在东非检测基孔肯雅热暴发的挑战。文章指出,传统的监测系统严重依赖临床病例检测,这会导致延迟,因为无症状感染和与其他疾病(如疟疾和登革热)症状重叠。分析指出,基孔肯雅热的传播模式已从森林型向城市型转变,涉及埃及伊蚊,特别是埃及伊蚊(Aedes aegypti)和白纹伊蚊(Aedes albopictus)。2019年在埃塞俄比亚的迪雷达瓦(Dire Dawa)记录了最大的基孔肯雅热暴发,共有41,162例疑似病例和16例实验室确诊病例。肯尼亚也曾在2004年和2016-2018年经历重大疫情,其中2004年的暴发攻击率为75%。文章强调需要改进监测系统,整合环境、媒介和流行病学数据,以预测暴发并指导预防措施。它呼吁采用One Health方法,不仅监测病毒本身,还监测其出现的驱动因素,如气候变化和城市化,以增强早期预警能力并加强跨境协调。

Mosquitoes, Climate Data Could Give East Africa Early Warning of Chikungunya - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
The article from Bioengineer.org, published on October 10, 2026, discusses the challenges of detecting chikungunya outbreaks in East Africa. It highlights that traditional surveillance systems rely heavily on clinical case detection, which introduces delays due to asymptomatic infections and overlapping symptoms with other diseases like malaria and dengue. The analysis notes that chikungunya transmission has shifted from sylvatic spillover to urban cycles involving Aedes mosquitoes, particularly Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus. The largest documented outbreak occurred in Dire Dawa, Ethiopia, in 2019, with 41,162 suspected cases and 16 confirmed cases. Kenya also experienced major epidemics in 2004 and 2016-2018, with an attack rate of 75% in the 2004 outbreak. The article emphasizes the need for improved surveillance systems that integrate environmental, vector, and epidemiological data to anticipate outbreaks and guide preventive actions. It calls for a One Health approach that monitors not only the virus but also the drivers of its emergence, such as climate change and urbanization, to enhance early warning capabilities and cross-border coordination.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 10, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Mosquitoes, Climate Data Could Give East Africa Early Warning of Chikungunya by October 10, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Mosquitoes, Climate Data Could Give East Africa Early Warning of Chikungunya Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Chikungunya virus has a deep historical connection to East Africa, the region where it was first identified, yet the surveillance systems meant to detect its return remain fundamentally reactive. According to a recent analysis published in New Microbes and New Infections, outbreak detection across the region is still anchored largely to the recognition and reporting of human illness. That dependence on clinical case detection introduces intrinsic delays: many infections produce no apparent symptoms, febrile illness caused by the virus overlaps with malaria, dengue and other common pathogens, and the standardization of surveillance varies widely between countries. The result is that outbreaks tend to appear sporadic and unpredictable, often recognized only after viral amplification is already underway in human and mosquito populations. The authors, a team of researchers led by Mohamed Abdulkadir Hussein, argue that this clinical anchoring is no longer adequate in a region where transmission patterns have shifted. Chikungunya increasingly spreads through urban and peri-urban human–mosquito cycles involving Aedes aegypti and, in some settings, Aedes albopictus, rather than arising solely from sylvatic spillover between forest mosquitoes and wild primates. Urban transmission cycles can amplify rapidly in dense city environments, which means the window for intervention between viral introduction and large-scale spread is narrow. Detection systems that only register the outbreak once patients fill clinics arrive too late to exploit that window. Discover more data Data Browse Reference The documented outbreak record illustrates both the scale of the threat and the unevenness of the data. The largest documented outbreak in the peer-reviewed evidence base occurred in Dire Dawa, Ethiopia, in 2019, with 41,162 suspected cases and 16 laboratory-confirmed cases in one outbreak report; a regional review using WHO-AFRO-linked surveillance synthesis describes the same event as involving more than 50,000 suspected cases. Kenya experienced major epidemics in 2004 and again in 2016 to 2018, with the 2004 outbreak on Lamu Island reaching an attack rate of 75 percent, one of the highest ever recorded for chikungunya. Djibouti confirmed a 2019 to 2020 urban outbreak, though the full citywide extent was not captured because of missing dedicated reporting; only a French Defense Community surveillance cohort reported 58 confirmed cases. Discover more Africa Check Forecasts Track Weather Shop African Art The gaps in that record are as revealing as the numbers themselves. For Tanzania, Uganda, Somalia, Rwanda, Burundi and South Sudan, extractable outbreak counts are largely absent, even though serologic evidence and reports of viral circulation exist for several of them. Reviews of African chikungunya transmission explicitly note disparities between reported cases, genomic data and dedicated reporting systems, and studies from Tanzania and Ethiopia emphasize inadequate laboratory capacity, under-recognition of cases and geographic heterogeneity in detection. In practical terms, a virus can be moving through mosquito populations and producing infections across entire provinces before any quantifiable signal reaches national or international reporting systems. The case for reform is sharpened by climate change. Climatic suitability for the mosquitoes that transmit chikungunya is increasing, with warmer conditions expanding the geographic range of Aedes vectors and lengthening transmission seasons. Experimental and field evidence shows that temperature, rainfall and humidity shape both mosquito abundance and transmission potential in measurable ways. Higher temperatures generally increase mosquito infection rates and transmission efficiency, because viral replication within the mosquito accelerates as ambient temperature rises. Rainfall increases Aedes abundance by expanding breeding sites, and precipitation and temperature extremes are strong predictors of habitat suitability and hotspot formation across the region. Discover more View Census Data Study Biology Store Big Data Recent outbreak analyses add a second layer to this climatic picture: heavy rainfall events combined with high temperatures can rapidly generate high vector densities and accelerate local spread within weeks. Broader climate-event cascades, in which droughts, floods and shifting rainfall patterns interact with urbanization, land use change and human mobility, intensify outbreak risk in ways that single-sector surveillance cannot capture. A public health ministry tracking only human cases sees the downstream consequence of these drivers; a system that also monitors the drivers themselves can anticipate them. This is the central technical argument for moving beyond pathogen-focused reporting toward surveillance of emergence. The proposed alternative is a One Health early warning architecture that explicitly integrates human, animal and environmental intelligence. One Health surveillance, as defined in the literature the authors cite, includes monitoring of the drivers of emergence rather than pathogens alone, combining clinical and laboratory diagnosis with vector, environmental, epidemiological and genomic surveillance. Operational examples from climate-sensitive vector-borne disease programs show that such integration can reveal overlooked transmission clusters, track viral diversity and guide preventive action before notifications peak. Regional networks have demonstrated that long-term multisectoral collaboration strengthens diagnostic capacity, vector monitoring, risk assessment and coordinated response across borders, a capability directly relevant to East Africa’s transboundary arboviral risk. Discover more Check Symptoms Buy Bug Kits Book Pest Control Buy Maps African Entomological surveillance is a critical component of this architecture, but the authors are careful to note its limitations. Field studies show that vector infection prevalence can be low and highly focal even when arboviruses are actively circulating, meaning that failure to detect virus in sampled mosquitoes does not imply absence of local risk. Nevertheless, vector surveillance still provides actionable early signals through metrics such as adult female density, egg counts, larval habitat mapping, vector distribution and risk stratification. In Mombasa, for example, entomological investigations detected chikungunya virus in Aedes vittatus while Aedes aegypti remained the dominant collected vector, showing how vector community composition itself carries information about transmission potential. Combined with epidemiological and environmental data, these indicators support targeted vector control and rational prioritization of scarce resources. Geography reinforces the integrated approach. Documented outbreaks cluster in coastal, urban and transport-linked settings such as Lamu, Mombasa, Kilifi, Dire Dawa and Djibouti City, locations where port traffic, trade corridors and rapid urban growth concentrate both human mobility and Aedes breeding habitat. The main periods of increased transmission in the region were the 2004 to 2005 East African resurgence centered on Kenya, the 2007 to 2008 period of Tanzanian activity, and the 2016 to 2020 wave that affected Mandera, Mombasa, Somalia, Ethiopia and Djibouti. Because these transmission chains follow movement and trade, an early warning system confined to one country or one data stream will systematically miss the cross-border seeding events that ignite new outbreaks. The policy conclusion the authors draw is unambiguous: ministries of health should move from passive case reporting toward regionally coordinated One Health surveillance platforms that link routine human case detection with mosquito monitoring and climate intelligence. Such platforms would require interoperable data systems, shared laboratory protocols and cross-border alert thresholds, allowing a signal detected in vector or climate data in one country to trigger preparatory action in neighboring ones. East Africa faces precisely the constraints this approach is designed to overcome: fragmented sectoral systems, weak data sharing, uneven laboratory capacity and rising exposure driven by mobility, urban growth and climate variability. Earlier detection of chikungunya, the analysis concludes, will depend less on counting human cases after spread has begun and more on integrating the entomological, environmental and climatic signals that precede them.
