蚊子的谱系影响地中海地区基孔肯雅热和登革热的风险

Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean
新闻简报一项发表在《Parasites & 媒介》上的新研究发现,地中海地区Aedes albopictus蚊子的遗传谱系显著影响其传播基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)、登革热病毒(DENV)和寨卡病毒(ZIKV)的能力。由Riccardo Piccinno和Anna Rodolfa Malacrida领导的研究结合了群体遗传学和实验媒介能力检测,表明即使地理位置相近,具有不同遗传背景的蚊群也表现出不同的传播能力。研究发现,对于CHIKV和DENV,遗传谱系和种群身份是感染、扩散和传播率的重要因素,而对于ZIKV则未发现显著预测因素。这些发现表明,统一的区域控制策略可能并不有效,因为传播潜力因当地蚊子的遗传特征而异。作者强调需要根据每个种群特定的传播风险制定本地化的媒介控制措施,而不是依赖于通用假设。该研究突出了将精细尺度的种群基因组学与标准化的媒介能力测试相结合的重要性,以更好地预测和管理欧洲地区的虫媒病毒风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年10月7日发表。研究结合了历史记录和人口模型分析地中海地区蚊群的遗传谱系,实验媒介能力检测在感染后第7、14和21天进行。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于地中海盆地,具体采集了瑞士南部、意大利北部和中部、巴尔干地区以及希腊的蚊子样本。分析中包括了一个位于中国广州的祖先参考种群。
事件
一项发表在《Parasites & 媒介》上的新研究探讨了地中海地区Aedes albopictus蚊群的遗传谱系如何影响其传播基孔肯雅病毒(CHIKV)、登革热病毒(DENV)和寨卡病毒(ZIKV)的能力。研究结果表明,遗传谱系而非地理位置的接近程度,显著影响这些病毒的媒介能力。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,遗传谱系和种群身份显著影响CHIKV和DENV的感染、扩散和传播率。对于ZIKV,未发现显著预测因素。该研究强调了由于蚊群遗传差异,不同地区可能面临不同的传播风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的发现基于群体遗传学和实验媒介能力检测。作者指出,蚊群的遗传异质性可能影响统一控制策略的有效性。研究建议在欧洲地区需要采用本地化、地理针对性的媒介控制策略。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean

News Brief
A new study published in Parasites & Vectors reveals that the genetic ancestry of Aedes albopictus mosquitoes in the Mediterranean significantly influences their ability to transmit chikungunya virus (CHIKV), dengue virus (DENV), and Zika virus (ZIKV). The research, led by Riccardo Piccinno and Anna Rodolfa Malacrida, combined population genetics and experimental vector competence assays to show that mosquito populations with distinct genetic backgrounds, even those geographically close, exhibit different transmission capabilities. The study found that for CHIKV and DENV, genetic ancestry and population identity were significant factors in infection, dissemination, and transmission rates, while no significant predictors were identified for ZIKV. The findings suggest that uniform regional control strategies may not be effective, as transmission potential varies based on local mosquito genetics. The authors emphasize the need for localized vector control measures calibrated to the specific transmission risks of each population, rather than relying on generic assumptions. The research underscores the importance of integrating fine-scale population genomics with standardized vector competence testing to better anticipate and manage arboviral risks in Europe.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, October 7, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean by October 7, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Mosquito ancestry shapes chikungunya and dengue risk across the Mediterranean Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram The Asian tiger mosquito, Aedes albopictus, has earned its reputation as one of the world’s most hazardous invasive species. Since first establishing itself in the Mediterranean Basin in the early 1980s, it has spread across southern Europe and become increasingly implicated in local, or autochthonous, transmission of chikungunya and dengue viruses in regions where these diseases were once exclusively travel-associated. A new study published in Parasites & Vectors now shows that the mosquito’s tangled colonization history is not just a curiosity of population genetics: the genetic ancestry of individual Mediterranean populations significantly shapes how competently they can acquire, disseminate and transmit chikungunya virus (CHIKV), dengue virus (DENV) and Zika virus (ZIKV), effectively redrawing the map of arboviral risk across Europe. The research, led by Riccardo Piccinno and Anna Rodolfa Malacrida of the University of Pavia, together with colleagues at the Institut Pasteur in Paris and partners in Switzerland, Italy and the Balkans, set out to answer a deceptively simple question: do genetically distinct mosquito populations differ in their ability to transmit arboviruses? The answer, the authors report, is a clear yes, and the pattern they uncovered is striking. Neighboring mosquito populations with different genetic ancestries showed drastically different competence profiles for CHIKV and DENV, while geographically distant populations that share ancestry behaved similarly. In other words, for these viruses, where a mosquito’s genes came from matters more than where the mosquito lives. To reach that conclusion, the team combined two complementary lines of evidence: population genetics and experimental vector competence assays. On the genetic side, they genotyped mosquitoes from an ancestral reference population in Guangzhou, China, and from ten adventive populations sampled across five Mediterranean regions, spanning Southern Switzerland, Northern and Central Italy, the Balkans and Greece. The markers of choice were highly polymorphic simple sequence repeats, or SSRs, which allow fine-scale discrimination of genetic lineages. Using the Bayesian clustering program STRUCTURE, the researchers assigned individuals to ancestry groups, and with the approximate Bayesian computation framework DIYABC-RF they reconstructed the demographic histories that produced the present-day genetic mosaic. That history, the study finds, was anything but a single clean invasion wave. By combining historical records with demographic modeling, the authors show that the establishment of Aedes albopictus in the Mediterranean was driven by chaotic, discontinuous introductions of distinct lineages, followed by admixture events in which previously separated genetic backgrounds interbred. The result is high heterogeneity in both space and ancestry: populations separated by only a few hundred kilometers can carry markedly different genetic compositions, reflecting separate introduction routes, likely via the international trade in used tires and other commodities that has repeatedly ferried mosquito eggs across borders. This genetic patchwork, rather than a homogeneous expansion front, is the biological backdrop against which viral transmission now plays out. The experimental half of the study measured vector competence using the standard trio of quantitative indices. Infection rate captures the proportion of mosquitoes in which the virus successfully establishes an infection in the midgut after an infectious blood meal; dissemination efficiency measures how many of those infected mosquitoes allow the virus to escape the midgut and spread to secondary organs, including the salivary glands; and transmission efficiency quantifies the fraction with virus present in the saliva, the prerequisite for onward transmission to a new host. Mosquitoes from each population were fed blood meals containing CHIKV strain 06.21, DENV-1, or ZIKV strain PE243, and these indices were scored at multiple days post-infection, specifically 7, 14 and 21 days, to track the temporal progression of infection. Statistical analysis then linked the genetic and virological datasets. Univariate and multivariate models assessed whether population identity, ancestry group membership, and days post-infection were associated with each competence index. For CHIKV, the analyses identified day post-infection, population, and two ancestry components as significant factors associated with infection, with some ancestry fractions showing positive associations and others negative ones. For DENV, population and three ancestry components were significantly associated with infection rate, although the authors note that two of these lacked a biologically plausible monotonic trend across ancestry categories, meaning higher ancestry fractions did not simply translate into higher infection rates. For ZIKV, by contrast, no variable emerged as a significant predictor, suggesting that the genetic heterogeneity captured in this study does not translate into measurable variation in Zika competence among these populations. The most consequential finding concerns CHIKV and DENV. Ancestry and population identity significantly influenced dissemination and transmission as well as infection, and the geographic pattern was counterintuitive: proximity did not predict similarity. Populations in neighboring geographical areas but with distinct ancestries exhibited drastically different competence profiles, whereas geographically distant populations sharing genetic ancestry displayed similar vector competence. This dissociation between geography and genetics implies that the introduction history of each local population, not local environmental conditions alone, has left a durable imprint on its capacity to transmit these viruses. It also suggests that the genetic variants governing viral infection and dissemination vary among source lineages, so that admixture has produced a mosaic of transmission potential across the region. The public health implications are direct. Europe has experienced a rising trend of both imported and autochthonous arboviral infections in recent years, with chikungunya and dengue outbreaks recorded in Italy, France and elsewhere. If the mosquitoes buzzing in one province are substantially more competent vectors than those in the next, then uniform, region-wide control assumptions may misallocate resources. The authors argue that their results underscore the urgent need for localized, geographically tailored vector control strategies in Europe, in which surveillance and intervention intensity are calibrated to the actual transmission potential of local mosquito populations rather than to a generic regional estimate. Methodologically, the study demonstrates the value of integrating fine-scale population genomics with standardized vector competence assays. The SSR-based genotyping, combined with STRUCTURE clustering and DIYABC-RF demographic inference, allowed the team to move beyond simple labels of origin and to quantify ancestry fractions within admixed populations. The logistic regression framework then made it possible to test the contribution of each factor while accounting for others, including the strong temporal effects of days post-infection that are well known to shape competence measurements. Supplementary analyses reported pairwise genetic differentiation among populations and detailed infection, dissemination and transmission rates stratified by virus, time point and population, providing a transparent record of the underlying data. The work also carries broader lessons for invasion biology. Aedes albopictus is a textbook example of how global trade creates repeated, genetically diverse introductions, and this study shows that such demographic chaos can have functional consequences for disease transmission. As the climate warms and the mosquito’s suitable habitat expands northward, new introductions and further admixture are likely, potentially reshaping vector competence in currently lower-risk areas. Understanding the provenance of each new population, the authors suggest, should therefore become part of the risk-assessment toolkit, alongside entomological surveillance and case monitoring, if Europe is to anticipate rather than merely react to the next arboviral emergence. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

卫生防护中心警告:蚊虫繁殖季期间登革热和基孔肯雅热风险增加

CHP Warns of Dengue and Chikungunya Risks Amid Ongoing Mosquito Breeding Season
新闻简报The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) of Hong Kong issued a warning about the risks of dengue fever (DF) and chikungunya fever (CF) amid the ongoing mosquito breeding season. The CHP reported one imported DF case and one imported CF case from October 2 to October 9, 2026. The CF patient had visited the Chinese Mainland during the incubation period and was infected during travel. Hong Kong has recorded 33 DF cases this year, including 31 imported and 2 local cases, while five CF cases have been confirmed, with four being imported and one local. Globally, DF cases have surged, with over 2.2 million cases and over 1,000 deaths reported from January to August 2026. The WHO noted 502,264 CF cases and 186 deaths in 41 countries last year. The CHP emphasized the need for public vigilance, urging measures such as removing mosquito breeding grounds, using DEET-containing repellents, and staying informed through official channels. The investigation into DF and CF is ongoing, and the CHP has provided detailed information on the situation in Hong Kong and neighboring regions.
查看新闻五要素
时间
CHP于2026年10月9日发布了关于登革热(DF)和基孔肯雅热(CF)的最新情况。最新登革热病例记录于2026年10月2日至10月9日,最新基孔肯雅热病例也记录于这一时期。世界卫生组织(WHO)报告了2026年1月至8月期间的全球登革热病例,以及去年在41个国家/地区报告的基孔肯雅热病例。
地点与对象
该报告关注香港及其周边地区,包括中国广东省,以及东南亚国家如越南、斯里兰卡、孟加拉国、柬埔寨、印度尼西亚和马来西亚。受影响人群包括香港居民和前往蚊媒疾病流行地区的旅行者。
事件
香港卫生防护中心(CHP)在持续的蚊虫繁殖季期间警告登革热(DF)和基孔肯雅热(CF)的风险。CHP记录显示,2026年10月2日至10月9日期间发现一例输入性登革热病例和一例输入性基孔肯雅热病例。该基孔肯雅热患者在潜伏期内曾前往中国大陆,并在旅行期间感染。
规模、影响与风险
Hong Kong has recorded 33 DF cases this year, including 31 imported cases and 2 local cases. For CF, Hong Kong has recorded five confirmed cases this year, including four imported cases and one local case. Globally, DF cases have increased significantly, with over 2.2 million cases and over 1,000 deaths reported from January to August 2026. The WHO reported 502,264 CF cases and 186 CF-related deaths in 41 countries/territories last year. The risk of importation persists, and the CHP considers DF and CF to be endemic in various countries worldwide.
应对、状态与不确定性
CHP的流行病学调查仍在进行中。该基孔肯雅热患者在无蚊虫环境中入院治疗,康复后已出院。其家庭接触者已完成医学观察,且无症状。CHP已将其网站上更新了关于最新登革热和基孔肯雅热情况的详细信息。预防措施包括保持环境卫生、使用含DEET的驱蚊剂以及清除潜在的蚊虫滋生地。

Skip to Content Javascript must be enabled for the correct page display CHP Warns of Dengue and Chikungunya Risks Amid Ongoing Mosquito Breeding Season News China Business TECH ENT Sport HK HotTV Asia Pacific Blog HK News China Business TECH ENT Sport HK HotTV Asia Pacific Blog Font Size A A Home HotNews NEWS CHINA BUSINESS TECH ENT SPORT HK ASIA PACIFIC BLOG HK HK CHP Warns of Dengue and Chikungunya Risks Amid Ongoing Mosquito Breeding Season 2026-10-09 17:30 Last Updated At:10-11 00:37 Facebook Weibo Update on mosquito-borne diseases, dengue fever and chikungunya fever The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) of the Department of Health today (October 9) announced the latest situation regarding dengue fever (DF) and chikungunya fever (CF). Although Hong Kong has entered autumn, the weather has yet to cool down and the rainy season has not yet ended, thus favourable for mosquito breeding. Members of the public are reminded to take appropriate measures to prevent mosquito bites and mosquito breeding, both in Hong Kong and when travelling to areas affected by mosquito-borne diseases, in order to prevent mosquito-borne diseases. Dengue fever ---------------- From October 2 to yesterday, the CHP recorded one imported DF case. The patient had visited Vietnam during the incubation period. Hong Kong has recorded 33 DF cases so far this year, comprising 31 imported cases and two local cases. A total of 59 DF cases were recorded last year (2025), all of which were imported cases. In 2024, 161 DF cases were recorded, comprising 156 imported cases and five local cases. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), the global incidence of DF has markedly increased over the past two decades, posing a substantial public health challenge. In 2024, the WHO recorded over 14 million DF cases, which was a record number. In 2025, over five million cases and over 4 000 DF-related deaths were reported in over 90 countries/territories. From January to August 2026, around 2.2 million cases and over 1 000 DF-related deaths have been recorded globally. DF has long been prevalent in many neighbouring tourist destinations. So far this year, Sri Lanka and Vietnam each recorded over 100 000 cases; Bangladesh and Cambodia each recorded over 80 000 cases; Indonesia and Malaysia each recorded over 70 000 cases; and Singapore recorded over 2 600 cases. Meanwhile, according to the Guangdong Provincial Disease Control and Prevention Administration, as of August 31, 2026, Guangdong Province has recorded 431 cases, of which 150 were recorded in August. Detailed information on the latest DF situation in Hong Kong, as well as neighbouring and overseas countries and areas, has been uploaded to the CHP's website. Chikungunya fever ----------------------- From October 2 to yesterday, the CHP recorded one imported CF case, involving a 73-year-old male. The patient had visited the Chinese Mainland during the incubation period. The CHP considered that he was infected during travel. The case was classified as an imported case. The patient had been admitted to the hospital for treatment in a mosquito-free environment. He recovered and discharged.His household contact has completed medical surveillance and is asymptomatic. The CHP's epidemiological investigation is ongoing. The case has been reported to the Chinese Mainland health authority. Hong Kong has recorded five confirmed CF cases so far this year, including four imported cases and one local case. Hong Kong recorded a total of 82 confirmed CF cases last year. Among them, 11 were local cases and the rest were imported cases. According to the WHO, a total of 502 264 CF cases and 186 CF-related deaths were reported in 41 countries/territories last year. Cases were reported in the Americas, Africa, Asia and Europe. According to the Guangdong Provincial Disease Control and Prevention Administration, as of August 31, 2026, Guangdong Province has recorded 739 cases, with 496 recorded in August, a sharp increase from the 36 and 171 cases recorded in June and July respectively. Preventive measures to be taken by the public ---------------------------------------------------- The CHP reminds members of the public to maintain good environmental hygiene, and to take appropriate measures to prevent mosquito bites and mosquito breeding, both in Hong Kong and when travelling to areas affected by mosquito-borne diseases, in order to prevent mosquito-borne diseases. Mosquito-borne diseases such as DF and CF are endemic in various countries worldwide. The risk of importation persists. Members of the public should check the situation at their destinations before travelling abroad, especially when travelling to Southeast Asian countries. Additionally, whether travelling or not, members of the public should use DEET-containing insect repellents or other effective active ingredients properly to prevent mosquito bites, but the following precautions should be taken when using them: read the label instructions carefully first; apply right before entering an area with a risk of mosquito bites; apply on exposed skin and clothing; use DEET of up to 30 per cent for pregnant women and up to 10 per cent for children (for children who travel to countries or areas where mosquito-borne diseases are endemic or epidemic, and where exposure is likely, those aged 2 months or above can use DEET-containing insect repellents with a DEET concentration of up to 30 per cent); apply sunscreen first, then insect repellent; reapply only when needed and follow the instructions; and in addition to DEET, there are other insect repellents available on the market containing different active ingredients, such as IR3535 and picaridin. When using any insect repellent, the public should follow the usage instructions and precautions on the product label. The Food and Environmental Hygiene Department (FEHD) appeals to members of the public to continue to stay alert and work together to carry out mosquito prevention and control measures early, including inspecting their homes and surroundings to remove potential breeding grounds, changing water in vases and scrubbing their inner surfaces, removing water in saucers under potted plants at least once a week, and properly disposing of containers such as empty cans and lunch boxes. The FEHD also advises members of the public and property management agencies to keep drains free of blockage and level all defective ground surfaces to prevent the accumulation of water. They should also scrub all drains and surface sewers with an alkaline detergent at least once a week to remove any mosquito eggs. The public should call 1823 in case of mosquito problems, and may visit the following pages for more information: the CF page of the CHP and the Travel Health Service, the latest Travel Health News, tips for using insect repellents, and the CHP Facebook Page, Instagram Account and YouTube Channel, and also the Mosquito Prevention and Control dedicated page of the FEHD. Valneva Shares Fall as UK Withdraws Approval for Ixchiq Chikungunya Vaccine - Yahoo Finance
新闻简报据雅虎财经报道,2026年10月8日,英国撤销了对Ixchiq寨卡病毒疫苗的批准。这一决定由英国人类药物委员会基于此前可获得的安全信息作出,且不会影响该疫苗在欧盟、加拿大、巴西或其他已批准地区的上市许可。法国开发商Valneva强调,此次撤销并非由于新发现的安全问题,而是基于从巴西及其他市场开展的试点项目中收集到的额外安全数据。全球范围内,已有大约187,000剂疫苗被接种。这一举措导致Valneva的股价下跌,如新闻报道所述。英国的决定凸显了预防基孔肯雅热的重要性,尤其是在对疫苗传播有利的蚊媒条件地区。

事件
据雅虎财经报道,英国撤销了对Ixchiq寨卡病毒疫苗的批准。该决定是基于英国人类药物委员会对之前可获得的安全信息进行评估作出的。
规模、影响与风险
撤销批准影响了Ixchiq在英国的市场推广,但不会影响其在欧盟、加拿大、巴西或其他市场的批准。全球范围内,已有大约187,000剂被接种。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Valneva Shares Fall as UK Withdraws Approval for Ixchiq Chikungunya Vaccine - Yahoo Finance

News Brief
On October 8, 2026, the UK withdrew approval for the Ixchiq chikungunya vaccine, as reported by Yahoo Finance. This decision was made by the UK's Commission on Human Medicines based on previously available safety information, and it does not affect the vaccine's marketing authorizations in the European Union, Canada, Brazil, or other regions where it has been approved. Valneva, the French developer, emphasized that the withdrawal was not prompted by new safety concerns but was informed by additional safety data collected from pilot programs in Brazil and other markets. Globally, approximately 187,000 doses of the vaccine have been administered. The move has led to a decline in Valneva's shares, as noted in the news report. The UK's decision highlights the importance of preventive measures against chikungunya, especially in regions with favorable mosquito conditions for transmission.

Corner More to Watch Must-watch Interviews Mag 7 Fed Watch Crypto Network AlphaSpace Get Finance Plus Valneva Shares Fall as UK Withdraws Approval for Ixchiq Chikungunya Vaccine Fiona Craig Thu, October 8, 2026 at 5:09 AM CDT 3 min read VALN +2.60% Medical research testing ©Pixnio Valneva (NASDAQ:VALN) shares declined on Monday after the UK Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency (MHRA) revoked the marketing authorisation for the company's Ixchiq chikungunya vaccine following a review of its safety and benefit-risk profile. The French vaccine developer said the decision followed an assessment by the UK's Commission on Human Medicines based on previously available safety information. The company emphasised that the regulatory action was not prompted by any newly identified safety concerns. The withdrawal applies specifically to the UK and does not affect Ixchiq's marketing authorisations in the European Union, Canada, Brazil or other markets where the vaccine has received approval. Valneva introduced Ixchiq in the UK in mid-2025, targeting travellers potentially exposed to the chikungunya virus. However, demand remained limited, and the company had already suspended active distribution in the country several months before the regulatory decision. According to Valneva, no Ixchiq doses currently remain on the UK market, meaning the company does not anticipate any expenses associated with a product recall. Management also expects the withdrawal to have only a minimal effect on future sales revenue, reflecting the vaccine's limited commercial presence in the UK. Valneva Maintains Positive Assessment of Ixchiq Safety Profile Despite the MHRA's decision, Valneva reiterated its position that Ixchiq maintains a favourable benefit-risk profile when administered in accordance with its approved indication. The company pointed to additional safety information gathered following the vaccine's introduction in several markets, including a pilot vaccination programme in Brazil. More than 61,000 people aged between 18 and 59 have received Ixchiq through the Brazilian initiative, with no new safety concerns identified, according to Valneva. Globally, approximately 187,000 doses of the vaccine have been administered. The company also highlighted recent regulatory assessments conducted by the European Medicines Agency (EMA) and Health Canada. Both authorities maintained their respective marketing authorisations for Ixchiq following safety reviews, although product information was updated to reflect the findings. These decisions allow the vaccine to remain available in those jurisdictions under the applicable regulatory conditions. Company Will Not Appeal UK Regulatory Decision Although Valneva disagrees with the conclusions reached by UK regulators, the company has decided against appealing the withdrawal of its marketing authorisation. Story Continues Management cited the limited financial and commercial significance of the UK market for Ixchiq, indicating that pursuing an appeal would not align with its current business priorities. Instead, Valneva intends to concentrate on expanding access to the vaccine in countries where chikungunya is endemic and the risk of infection is greater. The company considers these markets more important to its longer-term commercial strategy, particularly given the continuing spread of the virus across several regions. The decision to redirect resources towards endemic territories also reflects the relatively low demand for the vaccine among UK travellers since its launch. Chikungunya Vaccine Targets Mosquito-Borne Viral Disease Chikungunya is a viral infection transmitted through the bites of infected mosquitoes. The disease commonly causes fever, severe joint pain, muscle discomfort and other symptoms that can significantly affect patients. Since 2004, the virus has been identified in more than 110 countries across Asia, Africa, Europe and the Americas. The geographical spread of chikungunya has increased the importance of preventive measures, particularly in regions where mosquito populations and environmental conditions support transmission. Valneva's Ixchiq vaccine forms part of the company's strategy to address infectious diseases for which preventive options have historically been limited. While the UK authorisation withdrawal represents a regulatory setback, Valneva expects its direct financial impact to be minimal and plans to continue developing the vaccine's commercial presence in other approved markets. 浦那:市政公司向四名疟疾官员发出限期说明通知,因登革热和基孔肯雅热病例激增 - 浦那脉搏

Pune: PMC Issues Show-Cause Notices To Four Malaria Officers As Dengue, Chikungunya Cases Surge - PUNE PULSE
新闻简报浦那市政公司(PMC)已向四名疟疾官员发出限期说明通知,指控他们在各自辖区控制登革热和基孔肯雅热病例激增方面存在疏漏。通知已发给Kothrud、Nagar Road-Wadgaonsheri、Aundh-Baner和Dhankawadi-Sahakarnagar地区的官员,这些地区报告了大量登革热病例。2026年1月1日至9月30日,浦那记录了2,391例登革热病例和106例基孔肯雅热病例。在雨季期间,登革热病例急剧上升,9月达到956例,占总病例数的近40%。基孔肯雅热病例也有所增加,7月为51例,8月为39例,9月为16例。市政公司已加强媒介防控措施,包括逐户检查、容器调查和喷雾,在病例激增的地区开展这些活动。在报告了19例基孔肯雅热病例的Aundh-Baner地区,其中12例来自同一住宅社区,昆虫学调查显示该地区PMC仓库内有蚊虫繁殖。卫生部门已指示仓库当局清除相关材料并清理场地。官员们被传唤至PMC总部,并被要求提交详细说明其采取行动的报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
浦那:市政公司因登革热和基孔肯雅热病例激增向四名疟疾官员发出限期说明通知(2026年10月8日)。在雨季期间,登革热病例急剧上升,从6月的166例增至7月的518例、8月的751例和9月的956例。9月单独占了九个月期间报告的登革热病例总数的近40%。
地点与对象
浦那市,具体包括Kothrud、Nagar Road-Wadgaonsheri、Aundh-Baner和Dhankawadi-Sahakarnagar地区。基孔肯雅热病例也在Aundh-Baner地区报告,其中最高为19例,包括约12例来自同一住宅社区。
事件
浦那市政公司(PMC)卫生部门因指控四名疟疾官员在各自辖区未能有效控制登革热和基孔肯雅热病例激增,向其发出限期说明通知。官员们于周三被传唤至PMC总部,并被要求提交详细说明其采取行动的报告。
规模、影响与风险
浦那的登革热病例已超过2,300例,2026年1月1日至9月30日期间共记录了2,391例登革热病例和106例基孔肯雅热病例。在雨季期间,基孔肯雅热病例增加,7月为51例,8月为39例,9月为16例。卫生部门已指示官员在病例激增的地区实施加强的媒介防控措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
PMC已下令在病例激增地区实施加强的媒介防控措施,包括逐户检查、容器调查、发热调查、喷雾、疟疾血片采集和喷雾。这些活动将在出现暴发的情况下持续进行15天。昆虫学调查显示PMC仓库内有蚊虫繁殖,卫生部门已向仓库当局发出通知,要求清除相关材料并清理场地。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pune: PMC Issues Show-Cause Notices To Four Malaria Officers As Dengue, Chikungunya Cases Surge - PUNE PULSE

News Brief
The Pune Municipal Corporation (PMC) has issued show-cause notices to four malaria officers over alleged lapses in controlling the surge of dengue and chikungunya cases in their respective jurisdictions. The notices were issued to officers in Kothrud, Nagar Road-Wadgaonsheri, Aundh-Baner, and Dhankawadi-Sahakarnagar wards, which reported high numbers of dengue cases. Between January 1 and September 30, 2026, Pune recorded 2,391 dengue cases and 106 chikungunya cases. Dengue cases increased sharply during the monsoon, reaching 956 in September, which accounted for nearly 40% of the total cases. Chikungunya cases also rose, with 51 in July, 39 in August, and 16 in September. The PMC has intensified vector-control measures, including house-to-house inspections, container surveys, and fogging, in areas with outbreaks. In Aundh-Baner, where 19 chikungunya cases were reported, including 12 from a single housing society, an entomological survey found mosquito breeding at a PMC godown. The health department has directed the godown authorities to remove the material and clean the premises. The officers were summoned to the PMC headquarters and asked to submit reports detailing their actions.
Original source text (English)

The notices have been issued to malaria officers in the Kothrud, Nagar Road-Wadgaonsheri, Aundh-Baner and Dhankawadi-Sahakarnagar ward offices, which have reported a relatively high number of dengue cases. Dengue cases cross 2,300 According to PMC data, Pune recorded 2,391 dengue cases and 106 chikungunya cases between January 1 and September 30, 2026. WhatsApp Image 2026-08-21 at 10.58.36 AM VTP image Dengue cases increased sharply during the monsoon, rising from 166 in June to 518 in July, 751 in August and 956 in September. September alone accounted for nearly 40% of the total dengue cases reported during the nine-month period. WhatsApp Image 2026-08-21 at 10.59.27 AM WhatsApp Image 2026-08-21 at 10.59.27 AM Kothrud recorded the highest number of dengue cases within PMC limits at 229, followed by Nagar Road-Wadgaonsheri with 222 and Aundh-Baner with 212. Dhankawadi-Sahakarnagar reported 139 cases, while Yerawada-Kalas-Dhanori recorded 137. Chikungunya cases also increased during the monsoon. The city recorded 51 cases in July, 39 in August and 16 in September, taking the cumulative tally to 106. Discover more Book Pest Control News Subscription Service Epaper Access Aundh-Baner reported the highest number of chikungunya cases at 19, followed by Tilak Road-Sinhagad Road with four and Dhole-Patil Road with three. Officers asked to submit action-taken reports Dr Rajesh Dighe, assistant health chief and head of PMC’s vector-borne disease control programme, said the four officers have been asked to explain the reasons for the rise in cases and detail the measures taken in their respective areas. “The medical officers have been asked to submit a clarification on why the surge has been reported, what action has been taken, and the daily activities conducted to keep a check on cases in their areas,” Dighe said. The officers were summoned to the PMC headquarters on Wednesday and directed to submit reports detailing the action taken. PMC orders intensified vector-control measures The health department has directed officials to immediately implement outbreak-control measures in areas witnessing a surge in cases. These include house-to-house inspections within a five-kilometre radius, container surveys to identify mosquito breeding sites, fever surveys, spraying, malaria slide collection and fogging inside houses and surrounding areas. “In case of an outbreak, these activities will have to be carried out continuously for 15 days,” Dighe said. Action intensified in Aundh-Baner The PMC has also stepped up vector-control measures in Aundh-Baner after 19 chikungunya cases were reported, including around 12 cases from a single housing society. An entomological survey found mosquito breeding at a PMC godown belonging to the anti-encroachment department in the area. Large quantities of material had reportedly been dumped at the site, creating conditions conducive to mosquito breeding. The health department subsequently issued a notice to the godown authorities and directed them to remove the material and clean the premises. PMC teams also carried out large-scale application of biolarvicide using a jetting tanker, along with spraying and fogging at the affected housing society as part of the outbreak-control measures. The officers were summoned to the PMC headquarters on Wednesday and directed to submit reports detailing the action taken.
Scale, impact and risk
Dengue cases in Pune crossed 2,300, with a total of 2,391 cases and 106 chikungunya cases between January 1 and September 30, 2026. Chikungunya cases increased during the monsoon, with 51 cases in July, 39 in August, and 16 in September. The health department has directed officials to implement intensified vector-control measures in areas witnessing a surge in cases.
Response, status and uncertainty
PMC has ordered intensified vector-control measures, including house-to-house inspections, container surveys, fever surveys, spraying, malaria slide collection, and fogging. These activities will be carried out continuously for 15 days in case of an outbreak. An entomological survey found mosquito breeding at a PMC godown, and the health department issued a notice to the godown authorities to remove the material and clean the premises.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

2026-10 - 气候变化可能将蚊媒疾病推向非洲的新区域 - 沃茨大学

2026-10 - Climate change could push mosquito-borne diseases into new parts of Africa - Wits University
新闻简报A 2026-10-07 report by Wits University warns that climate change is expanding the range of disease-carrying mosquitoes, including those that transmit chikungunya virus, into new parts of Africa. Professor Helen Rees, a public health expert, highlighted that warmer conditions are altering mosquito habitats, leading to longer transmission seasons and increased risk for African cities. She specifically mentioned the spread of Anopheles stephensi, a malaria-carrying mosquito, and Aedes mosquitoes, which spread dengue, yellow fever, chikungunya, and Zika. The report notes that these mosquitoes thrive in urban environments and breed in man-made water containers. Insecticide resistance and diagnostic challenges further complicate the threat. Professor Dick Chamla from WHO AFRO emphasized that Africa’s health systems are only about 50% prepared for these overlapping climate and health risks, far below the optimal 80% level. The warning comes amid a strong El Niño event and long-term global warming, which are expected to increase drought, heat, and flood risks across the continent. Climate science offers an opportunity for governments to prepare and implement early-warning systems and adaptation strategies to mitigate the impact of these emerging health threats.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026-10 - 气候变化可能将蚊媒疾病推向非洲的新区域 - 沃茨大学 About People/Staff Alumni Library Visit Wits Give Apply Homepage Study at Wits Students Faculties and Schools Teaching and Learning Research and Innovation News Search Search the site Search Menu Search Search the site Search Start main page content Home News Latest news Research news 2026 2026-10 气候变化可能将蚊媒疾病推向非洲的新区域 7 October 2026 - 沃茨大学 警告正值南部非洲面临特别危险的强厄尔尼诺现象和长期全球变暖的组合。
地点与对象
南部非洲、东部非洲和非洲城市。
事件
气候变化正在扩大携带疾病的蚊子的分布范围,包括传播基孔肯雅病毒的蚊子,进入非洲的新区域。这一警告由沃茨大学公共卫生专家海伦·里斯教授提出。
规模、影响与风险
按蚊Anopheles stephensi以及传播登革热、黄热病、基孔肯雅热和寨卡病毒的伊蚊Aedes的扩散,可能会使更多非洲城市居民暴露于这些疾病之中。风险显著,可能增加传播的传播,并因杀虫剂抗性和诊断困难而面临更多挑战。
应对、状态与不确定性
该警告强调了建立预警系统、适应性规划以及保护易感社区的必要性。世卫组织非洲区域办事处的迪克·查姆拉教授指出,非洲的卫生系统尚未完全准备就绪,其准备能力约为50%,远低于理想水平。

2026-10 - Climate change could push mosquito-borne diseases into new parts of Africa - Wits University

News Brief
A 2026-10-07 report by Wits University warns that climate change is expanding the range of disease-carrying mosquitoes, including those that transmit chikungunya virus, into new parts of Africa. Professor Helen Rees, a public health expert, highlighted that warmer conditions are altering mosquito habitats, leading to longer transmission seasons and increased risk for African cities. She specifically mentioned the spread of Anopheles stephensi, a malaria-carrying mosquito, and Aedes mosquitoes, which spread dengue, yellow fever, chikungunya, and Zika. Original source text (English)
2026-10 - Climate change could push mosquito-borne diseases into new parts of Africa - Wits University About People/Staff Alumni Library Visit Wits Give Apply Homepage Study at Wits Students Faculties and Schools Teaching and Learning Research and Innovation News Search Search the site Search Menu Search Search the site Search Start main page content Home News Latest news Research news 2026 2026-10 Climate change could push mosquito-borne diseases into new parts of Africa 7 October 2026 - Wits University Warning comes as Southern Africa is faced with a particularly dangerous combination of a strong El Niño and long-term global warming. Climate change is expanding the range of disease-carrying mosquitoes and could expose more people in African cities to malaria, dengue, yellow fever, chikungunya and Zika, Wits public health expert Professor Helen Rees has warned. Speaking at the African Academy of Sciences Presidential Lecture Series on climate change and health, Rees said warmer conditions are changing where mosquitoes can survive, how long transmission seasons last and which populations are at risk. “What we’re seeing is expanded mosquito ranges both to higher latitudes and altitudes,” she said. “There’s lengthening of transmission seasons in some of the regions.” Rees highlighted the spread of Anopheles stephensi, a malaria-carrying mosquito originally from South Asia and the Arabian Peninsula, which is now expanding across the Horn of Africa and into African cities. “It thrives in urban environments and it breeds in water storage containers and other man-made habitats,” she said. The mosquito can transmit both major forms of malaria, while growing insecticide resistance and difficulties with some rapid diagnostic tests add to the threat. Rees also warned that Aedes mosquitoes, which spread dengue, yellow fever, chikungunya and Zika are likely to expand their distribution as temperatures rise. “The spread that we could potentially see of these infectious diseases could be very, very significant in the African region,” Rees said. Her warning came as Wits climate scientist and Director of the Wits Global Change Institute, Professor François Engelbrecht, said Southern Africa faced a particularly dangerous combination of a strong El Niño and long-term global warming. Southern Africa is likely to experience below-normal rainfall and drought, while East Africa faced a greater chance of heavy rainfall and flooding. Engelbrecht said the Mozambique Channel is now warm enough to sustain an extremely powerful tropical cyclone, with evidence pointing to the possibility of a Category 5 landfall in Mozambique within the next decade. The coming summer could bring unprecedented heat to Southern Africa. El Niño is also giving the continent a glimpse of what a warmer future may look like. Evidence suggests stronger El Niño events could occur more frequently, meaning Africa will need to prepare for repeated drought, heat and flood risks. Climate science, however, gives governments an opportunity to act before disaster strikes. “We have a chance to prepare because the climate models help us to anticipate what the main risks are,” Engelbrecht said, adding that early-warning systems, adaptation planning and protecting vulnerable communities will become increasingly important. WHO AFRO’s Professor Dick Chamla said Africa’s health systems are not yet fully prepared for these overlapping threats. Preparedness capacity across Africa remains at about 50%, well below the roughly 80% level considered optimal. Share 19 3 Close ShareThis Copy and Paste Chat with me Kudubot Reply in 10 seconds. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

佛罗里达州登革热病例激增至296例,为数十年来美国最大暴发 - 立陶宛赫尔德-特里布恩报

Florida dengue cases surge to 296, largest US outbreak in decades - Sarasota Herald-Tribune
新闻简报佛罗里达州正在经历登革热病例的显著激增,截至2026年10月3日,已报告296例本地获得性病例,这是美国数十年来最大的暴发。其中259例集中在希尔斯伯勒县,该县确认有一例因登革热感染、败血性休克和多器官衰竭导致的死亡。此次暴发促使16个县发布蚊媒疾病警报或建议,并有多个县宣布紧急状态以加快蚊虫控制工作。佛罗里达州卫生部将病例激增归因于气候因素,如气温升高、降雨量增加以及积水,以及来自登革热暴发国家的人类旅行,特别是古巴。虽然大多数病例是本地获得的,但也报告了262例与国际旅行相关的病例,其中215例来自古巴。该州继续通过与当地蚊虫控制合作伙伴的协调努力监测和应对此次暴发。登革热不具传染性,而是通过蚊虫传播,症状包括剧烈疼痛、发烧,严重情况下可能导致危及生命的并发症。此次暴发凸显了提高公众意识和采取预防措施的必要性,以减少蚊虫叮咬并限制疾病传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
此次暴发报告截至2026年10月9日,病例激增始于三个月前。最新更新提到截至2026年10月3日的全州总病例数,以及一周内报告的44例新病例。
地点与对象
此次暴发主要集中在佛罗里达州,特别是希尔斯伯勒县,该县有259例确诊病例。其他受影响的县包括帕斯科县、皮尼拉斯县、迈阿密-戴德县、橙县、布劳沃德县、棕榈滩县和波克县。受影响的人群包括这些县的居民,其中一些病例与国际旅行有关,尤其是来自古巴的病例。
事件
佛罗里达州今年报告了296例本地获得性登革热病例,标志着美国数十年来最大的暴发。佛罗里达州卫生部确认希尔斯伯勒县有一例因登革热感染、败血性休克和多器官衰竭导致的死亡。
规模、影响与风险
已报告296例本地获得性登革热病例,其中259例在希尔斯伯勒县。确认有一例死亡。此外,还报告了262例与国际旅行相关的登革热病例,其中215例来自古巴。此次暴发促使16个县发布蚊媒疾病警报或建议。
应对、状态与不确定性
多个县已宣布紧急状态以加快蚊虫控制工作。佛罗里达州卫生部继续与当地蚊虫控制合作伙伴协调,开展针对性的应对措施。此次暴发被归因于气候和人为因素,包括气温升高、降雨量增加以及来自其他登革热暴发国家的人类旅行。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida dengue cases surge to 296, largest US outbreak in decades - Sarasota Herald-Tribune

News Brief
Florida is experiencing a significant surge in dengue cases, with 296 locally acquired cases reported as of October 3, 2026, marking the largest outbreak in the United States in decades. The majority of these cases, 259, are concentrated in Hillsborough County, where one death was confirmed due to dengue infection, septic shock, and multi-organ failure. The outbreak has prompted 16 counties to issue mosquito-borne illness alerts or advisories, and several have declared states of emergency to accelerate mosquito control efforts. The Florida Department of Health attributes the surge to climatic factors such as warmer temperatures, increased rainfall, and standing water, along with human travel from countries with dengue outbreaks, particularly Cuba. While the majority of cases are locally acquired, 262 cases linked to international travel were also reported, with 215 from Cuba. The state continues to monitor and respond to the outbreak through coordinated efforts with local mosquito control partners. Dengue is not contagious but is spread by mosquitoes, and symptoms include severe pain, fever, and in severe cases, life-threatening complications. The outbreak highlights the need for increased public awareness and preventive measures to reduce mosquito bites and limit disease transmission.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Ticket Lifestyle Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals NEWS Florida dengue cases surge to 296, largest US outbreak in decades State adds 44 more cases C. A. Bridges Cheryl McCloud USA TODAY NETWORK - Florida Oct. 9, 2026, 10:44 a.m. ET Florida has reported 296 locally acquired dengue fever cases this year, the largest outbreak in the United States in decades. The majority of cases, 259, have been confirmed in Hillsborough County, where one death has been reported. Several counties have declared states of emergency to expedite mosquito control, with 16 counties under a mosquito-borne illness alert or advisory. Florida reported 44 new locally acquired cases of dengue fever in one week, according to the latest update from the Florida Department of Health. The disease is also called breakbone fever, due to the pain it causes. That brings the statewide total to 296 for the year as of Oct. 3, the largest outbreak in the United States in decades. The previous highest number of Florida cases in recent memory was 199 in 2023. In 2025, Florida reported 62 cases for the year. Shop Amazon for mosquito repellents The bulk of the cases have been in Hillsborough County, which has 259 confirmed cases, while other locally acquired cases have been reported in seven other counties. Broward County reported its first confirmed human dengue case on Oct. 5. One death was confirmed in Hillsborough County, identified as 80-year-old Gay Small, who died of “dengue infection” along with septic shock and multi-organ failure, according to a death certificate. The previous largest outbreak was in Hawaii's Big Island, where 264 local cases were reported in the six months between September 2015 and March 2016. Florida's surge began three months ago. ➤ Prevent dengue at home in Florida with this 10-minute mosquito inspection Three Tampa Bay-area counties — Pasco, Hillsborough, and Pinellas — have declared states of emergency to expedite mosquito control operations and cut through paperwork for emergency response. Currently, 16 counties are under either a mosquito-borne illness advisory or mosquito-borne illness alert. West Nile virus and chikungunya — which are also transmitted by mosquitoes — have also been reported in Florida this year, but in far fewer numbers than dengue. Another human case of West Nile was reported in Holmes County in the period of Sept. 27 - Oct. 3, bringing the state total to seven for the year. No new chikungunya cases were reported. ➤ Dengue cases keep climbing in Florida. This is how to protect yourself There have also been 262 dengue cases associated with international travel reported in Florida this year, 215 of which were from travel from Cuba, according to the FDOH. Most of the cases were reported in Miami-Dade County. Although these cases were not locally acquired, mosquitoes that bite infected people in the state can transmit the disease to others. Here's what you should know about mosquito-borne illnesses in Florida, symptoms to watch for, and how best to prevent mosquito bites, including advice from the Florida Department of Health. Dengue still surging in Florida. See counties reporting mosquito-borne illnesses According to the Florida Department of Health, as of Oct. 3, there have been 296 cases of locally acquired cases of dengue reported in seven counties, which is up from 252 reported Oct. 1. There have also been seven positive samples of West Nile virus and one case of chikungunya. Dengue: 296 cases 259 in Hillsborough County 20 in Pinellas County 8 in Miami-Dade County 3 in Orange County 3 in Pasco County 1 in Broward County 1 in Palm Beach County 1 in Polk County West Nile: 7 cases 2 in Alachua County 2 in Duval County (1 an asymptomatic blood donor) 1 in Holmes County 1 in Leon (asymptomatic blood donor) 1 in Walton County Chikungunya: 1 case 1 in Miami-Dade County What's causing the Florida outbreak of dengue fever? One reason for the surge in dengue cases is due to a comibation of "climatic and human factors that create ideal conditions" for the types of mosquitoes that spread dengue, West Nile and the chikungunya viruses, said Eva Buckner, associate professor and extension specialist with the UF/IFAS Florida Medical Entomology Laboratory via email Sept. 23. Those factors include warmer temperatures, increased rainfall and standing water, and human travel from other countries with outbreaks. Health data show that 215 of the 262 Florida cases acquired from outside the country were people who had recently traveled from Cuba. Most of the cases were DENV-2, one of the disease's four serotypes and the serotype behind Cuba's current epidemic, where fuel shortages are hampering fumigation efforts, according to Forbes. 16 mosquito-borne illness alerts, advisories issued for these Florida counties A mosquito-borne illness advisory has been issued for the following counties: Brevard Broward Holmes Leon Manatee Martin Palm Beach Polk Walton A mosquito-borne illness alert has been issued for the following counties: Alachua Duval Hillsborough Miami-Dade Orange Pasco Pinellas "A mosquito-borne illness advisory means the public should take precautions to reduce mosquito bites due to factors indicating local disease such as positive sentinels, very few presumptive or positive human cases, or other justifications," according to Sarasota County. "A mosquito-borne illness alert means that there has been heightened disease activity, a significant increase in vector (disease capable) mosquito populations, or an increase in locally-transmitted human cases. "Residents should continue to take precautions, but exercise additional caution in preventing mosquito bites." All Florida residents could take precautions to avoid mosquito-borne illnesses "Residents throughout the state should continue to take precautions from mosquito-borne illnesses by appropriately applying bug spray, avoiding areas with high mosquito populations, and wearing long pants and shirts when possible — especially during sunrise and sunset when mosquitos are most active," said Isabella White, FDOH press secretary via email Sept. 23. White said FDOH "continues to coordinate with local mosquito control partners to support surveillance and targeted response efforts in affected areas. These efforts help reduce mosquito populations, limit the risk of disease transmission, and protect residents across Florida." What are symptoms of dengue, breakbone fever? "Dengue fever can be a painful, debilitating disease but is rarely fatal," the Florida Department of Health said. Symptoms appear three to 14 days after the bite of an infected mosquito and include: sudden onset of fever severe headache eye pain muscle and joint pain (giving the disease the nickname “breakbone fever”) bleeding Gastrointestinal symptoms like vomiting and diarrhea may also be present in some cases. "Dengue fever symptoms usually last four to seven days. The disease is often diagnosed incorrectly because the symptoms are similar to influenza and other viruses," the CDC said. "Around one out of every 20 people infected by a dengue virus develops severe dengue, a life-threatening medical emergency that requires immediate medical attention," according to YaleMedicine. Why is dengue called 'breakbone fever?' Dengue is sometimes called “breakbone fever” because, in addition to fever, "it can cause severe aches and pains, especially in the legs, joints, and back," according to YaleMedicine. Dengue does not actually cause bones to break, according to YaleMedicine. Is dengue contagious? Dengue is spread by mosquitoes. Mosquitoes become infected with dengue after biting a person with the virus. Those infected mosquitoes then spread the virus to other people through bites. The Centers for Disease Control said a woman infected with the dengue virus during pregnancy could pass the virus to her fetus, causing death to the fetus, low birth weight, or premature birth. Can dengue be treated? "While there is no cure for dengue and there are no specific medications for the condition, getting adequate hydration and plenty of rest, and taking acetaminophen for pain and fever can help alleviate symptoms while the body’s immune system fights the virus," according to YaleMedicine. "In most cases, people recover from dengue within a week. For those with severe dengue, treatment by health care professionals can significantly lower the risk of death." What are the best mosquito repellents to use? According to the Florida Department of Health, repellents with the following active ingredients provide the best protection: DEET Picaridin IR3535 Oil of lemon eucalyptus Para-menthane-diol 2-undecanone, a plant-derived ingredient 'Drain and cover' helps keep mosquitoes away The FDOH offered this advice to help keep mosquitoes away from your Florida home: Drain Drain water from garbage cans, old tires, pool covers, or any other containers where water collects. Empty and clean birdbaths and pet water bowls at least once or twice a week. Protect boats and vehicles from rain with tarps that don’t accumulate water. Maintain swimming pools in good condition and ensure they are appropriately chlorinated. Empty plastic swimming pools when not in use. Cover If you must be outside when mosquitoes are active, cover up. Apply mosquito repellent to exposed skin and clothing. Use mosquito netting to protect children younger than 2 months old. C.A. Time
The outbreak was reported as of October 9, 2026, with the surge beginning three months prior. The latest update mentions the statewide total as of October 3, 2026, and the 44 new cases reported in one week.
Location and population
The outbreak is primarily in Florida, specifically Hillsborough County, which has 259 confirmed cases. Other affected counties include Pasco, Pinellas, Miami-Dade, Orange, Broward, Palm Beach, and Polk. The affected population includes residents of these counties, with some cases linked to international travel, particularly from Cuba.
Event
Florida has reported 296 locally acquired dengue fever cases this year, marking the largest outbreak in the United States in decades. The Florida Department of Health confirmed one death in Hillsborough County due to dengue infection, septic shock, and multi-organ failure.
Scale, impact and risk
There have been 296 locally acquired dengue cases, with 259 in Hillsborough County. One death was confirmed. Additionally, 262 dengue cases associated with international travel were reported, with 215 from Cuba. The outbreak has prompted 16 counties to issue mosquito-borne illness alerts or advisories.
Response, status and uncertainty
Several counties have declared states of emergency to expedite mosquito control. The Florida Department of Health continues to coordinate with local mosquito control partners for surveillance and targeted response efforts. The outbreak is attributed to climatic and human factors, including warmer temperatures, increased rainfall, and human travel from other countries with outbreaks.

What Purposes Do Mosquitoes Serve in Ecosystems? | Pollination, Food Webs, Ecology, & Facts - Britannica
